**ἡμέτερος, α, ον** our, our own.

## ἡμιθανής
**ἡμιθανής, ές** half-dead.

## ἥμισυς
**ἥμισυς, εια, υ** half.

## ἡμιώριον
**ἡμιώριον, ου, τό** half an hour.

## ἡνίκα
**ἡνίκα** when, whenever, at which time.

## ἤπερ
**ἤπερ** than.

## ἤπιος
**ἤπιος, α, ον** placid, gentle, mild.

## Ἤρ
**Ἤρ, ὁ** Er, son of Joshua and father of Elmadam.

## ἤρεμος
**ἤρεμος, ον** quiet, tranquil.

## Ἡρῴδης
**Ἡρῴδης, ου, ὁ** Herod; four persons are called by this name: Herod the Great, Herod Antipas, Herod Agrippa, and Herod Agrippa the younger.

## Ἡρῳδιανοί
**Ἡρῳδιανοί, ῶν, οἱ** the Herodians, the partisans of Herod (Antipas).

## Ἡρῳδιάς
**Ἡρῳδιάς, άδος, ἡ** Herodias (died after A.D. 40), daughter of Aristobulus and granddaughter of Herod I, wife, first, of her uncle Herod, second, of his half-brother, her uncle Herod Antipas.

## Ἡρῳδίων
**Ἡρῳδίων, ωνος, ὁ** Herodion, a Christian in Rome, a relative of Paul.

## Ἠσαΐας
**Ἠσαΐας, ου, ὁ** Isaiah, the prophet.

## Ἠσαῦ
**Ἠσαῦ, ὁ** Esau, elder son of Isaac the patriarch, brother of Jacob.

## ἥσσων
**ἧττων, ον** lesser, inferior, weaker.

## ἡσυχάζω
**ἡσυχάζω** I rest from work, cease from altercation, am silent, live quietly.

## ἡσυχία
**ἡσυχία, ας, ἡ** quietness, stillness, silence.

## ἡσύχιος
**ἡσύχιος, α, ον** quiet, tranquil, peaceful.

## ἤτοι
**ἤτοι** whether.

## ἡττάομαι
**ἡττάομαι** I am defeated, am overcome, am made inferior.

## ἥττημα
**ἥττημα, ατος, τό** defect, loss, defeat, failure, shortcoming.

## ἠχέω
**ἠχέω** I make a sound, give forth a sound, sound (when struck); I roar (as the sea).

## ἦχος
**ἦχος, ου, ὁ** (a) a sound, noise, (b) a rumor, report.

## θά
**μαρὰν ἀθά** (Aramaic), either: Our Lord hath come, or: Our Lord cometh (will come, is at hand).

## Θαδδαῖος
**Θαδδαῖος, ου, ὁ** Thaddaeus, one of the twelve apostles.

## Θάλασσα
**θάλασσα, ης, ἡ** (a) the sea, in contrast to the land, (b) a particular sea or lake, e.g. the sea of Galilee (Tiberias), the Red Sea.

## θάλασσα
**θάλασσα, ης, ἡ** (a) the sea, in contrast to the land, (b) a particular sea or lake, e.g. the sea of Galilee (Tiberias), the Red Sea.

## θάλπω
**θάλπω** (properly: I warm, then) I cherish, nourish, foster, comfort.

## Θαμάρ
**Θάμαρ, ἡ** Tamar, mother of Perez and Zerah by Judah, son of Jacob.

## θαμβέομαι
**θαμβέω** I amaze; pass: I am amazed (almost terrified).

## θάμβος
**θάμβος, ους, τό** astonishment, amazement (allied to terror or awe).

## θανάσιμον
**θανάσιμος, ον** deadly, mortal, fatal.

## θανατηφόρος
**θανατηφόρος, ον** death-bringing, deadly.

## θάνατος
**θάνατος, ου, ὁ** death, physical or spiritual.

## θανατόω
**θανατόω** I put to death, subdue; pass: I am in danger of death, am dead to, am rid of, am parted from.

## θάπτω
**θάπτω** I bury.

## Θάρα
**Θάρα, ὁ** Terah, the father of Abraham.

## θαρρέω
**θαρρέω** I am courageous, confident, of good cheer.

## θαρσέω
**θαρσέω** I am of good courage, good cheer, am bold.

## θάρσος
**θάρσος, ους, τό** courage, confidence.

## θαῦμα
**θαῦμα, ατος, τό** (a) concr: a marvel, wonder, (b) abstr: wonder, amazement.

## θαυμάζω
**θαυμάζω** (a) intrans: I wonder, marvel, (b) trans: I wonder at, admire.

## θαυμάσιος
**θαυμάσιος, α, ον** wonderful, admirable; subst: a wonder.

## θαυμαστός
**θαυμαστός, ή, όν** to be wondered at, wonderful, marvelous.

## θεά
**θεά, ᾶς, ἡ** a goddess.

## θεάομαι
**θεάομαι** I see, behold, contemplate, look upon, view; I see, visit.

## θεατρίζω
**θεατρίζω** I make a public show of, expose to public shame.

## θέατρον
**θέατρον, ου, τό** (a) a theatre, a semi-circular stone building, generally open to the sky, (b) a spectacle, show.

## θεῖον
**θεῖον, ου, τό** brimstone, sulfur.

## θεῖος
**θεῖος, α, ον** divine; subst: the Deity.

## θειότης
**θειότης, ητος, ἡ** divinity, divine nature.

## θειώδης
**θειώδης, ες** of brimstone, sulfurous.

## θέλημα
**θέλημα, ατος, τό** an act of will, will; plur: wishes, desires.

## θέλησις
**θέλησις, εως, ἡ** a willing, will.

## θέλω
**θέλω** I will, wish, desire, am willing, intend, design.

## θεμέλιον
**θεμέλιος, ον** (properly, an adj: belonging to the foundation), a foundation stone.

## θεμέλιος
**θεμέλιος, ον** (properly, an adj: belonging to the foundation), a foundation stone.

## θεμελιόω
**θεμελιόω** I found, lay the foundation (lit. and met.).

## θεοδίδακτος
**θεοδίδακτος, ον** taught by God, divinely instructed.

## θεομάχος
**θεομάχος, ον** fighting against God.

## θεόπνευστος
**θεόπνευστος, ον** God-breathed, inspired by God, due to the inspiration of God.

## θεός
**θεός, οῦ, ὁ** (a) God, (b) a god, generally.

## θεοσέβεια
**θεοσέβεια, ας, ἡ** reverence for God, fear of God, godliness, piety.

## θεοσεβής
**θεοσεβής, ές** devout, pious, God-fearing.

## θεοστυγής
**θεοστυγής, ές** hating God, hateful to God.

## θεότης
**θεότης, ητος, ἡ** deity, Godhead.

## Θεόφιλος
**Θεόφιλος, ου, ὁ** Theophilus, a friend of Luke of equestrian rank, to whom the Gospel and Acts are dedicated.

## θεραπεία
**θεραπεία, ας, ἡ** care, attention, especially medical attention (treatment); hence almost: healing; meton: those who render service.

## θεραπεύω
**θεραπεύω** I care for, attend, serve, treat, especially of a physician; hence: I heal.

## θεράπων
**θεράπων, οντος, ὁ** a servant, attendant, minister.

## θερίζω
**θερίζω** I reap, gather, harvest.

## θερισμός
**θερισμός, οῦ, ὁ** reaping, harvest; met: the harvest, crop.

## θεριστής
**θεριστής, οῦ, ὁ** a reaper, harvester.

## θερμαίνομαι
**θερμαίνω** I warm; mid: I warm myself.

## θέρμη
**θέρμη, ης, ἡ** heat.

## θέρος
**θέρος, ους, τό** summer.

## Θεσσαλονικεύς
**Θεσσαλονικεύς, έως, ὁ** a Thessalonian.

## Θεσσαλονίκη
**Θεσσαλονίκη, ης, ἡ** Thessalonica (modern Saloniki), an important city of the Roman province Macedonia.

## Θευδᾶς
**Θευδᾶς, ᾶ, ὁ** Theudas, a Jewish pretender of date about 4 B.C., otherwise unknown.

## θεωρέω
**θεωρέω** I look at, gaze, behold; I see, experience, discern; I partake of.

## θεωρία
**θεωρία, ας, ἡ** a sight, spectacle.

## θήκη
**θήκη, ῆς, ἡ** a repository, receptacle; a case, sheath, scabbard.

## θηλάζω
**θηλάζω** (a) I give suck, (b) I suck.

## θῆλυς
**θῆλυς, εια, υ** female.

## θήρα
**θήρα, ας, ἡ** hunting, entrapping; prey, game; a net, means of capture.

## θηρεύω
**θηρεύω** I hunt, seek to catch or entrap; met: I lay hold of.

## θηριομαχέω
**θηριομαχέω** I fight with wild beasts (i.e. wild beasts in human form); met: I am exposed to fierce hostility.

## θηρίον
**θηρίον, ου, τό** properly: a wild beast, hence: any animal; met: a brute.

## θησαυρίζω
**θησαυρίζω** I store up, treasure up, save, lay up.

## θησαυρός
**θησαυρός, οῦ, ὁ** a store-house for precious things; hence: a treasure, a store.

## θιγγάνω
**θιγγάνω** I touch, handle, injure, harm.

## θλίβω
**θλίβω** (a) I make narrow (strictly: by pressure); I press upon, (b) I persecute, press hard.

## θλῖψις
**θλῖψις, εως, ἡ** persecution, affliction, distress, tribulation.

## θνῄσκω
**θνῄσκω** I die, am dying, am dead.

## θνητός
**θνητός, ή, όν** mortal, subject to death.

## θορυβάζω
**τυρβάζω** I agitate or disturb in mind, trouble.

## θορυβέω
**θορυβέω** I disturb greatly, terrify, strike with panic; mid: I show agitation of mind.

## θόρυβος
**θόρυβος, ου, ὁ** (a) din, hubbub, confused noise, outcry, (b) riot, disturbance.

## θραύω
**θραύω** I crush, break, shatter; met: I break down.

## θρέμμα
**θρέμμα, ατος, τό** (lit: a nursling, hence probably) plur: cattle.

## θρηνέω
**θρηνέω** intrans: I lament, wail; trans: I bewail.

## θρησκεία
**θρησκεία, ας, ἡ** (underlying sense: reverence or worship of the gods), worship as expressed in ritual acts, religion.

## θρησκός
**θρῆσκος, ον** (refers probably to a careful observance of religious restrictions), religious (probably in a limited sense), devout.

## θριαμβεύω
**θριαμβεύω** (properly: I lead one as my prisoner in a triumphal procession, hence) I lead around, make a show (spectacle) of, cause to triumph.

## θρίξ
**θρίξ, τριχός, ἡ** hair (of the head or of animals).

## θροέομαι
**θροέω** I disturb, agitate; pass: I am troubled, alarmed.

## θρόμβος
**θρόμβος, ου, ὁ** a clot, large drop (of blood).

## θρόνος
**θρόνος, ου, ὁ** a (king's) throne, seat; meton: power, dominion; a potentate.

## Θυάτειρα
**Θυάτειρα, ων, τά** Thyatira, a city of the old district Lydia, in the Roman province Asia.

## θυγάτηρ
**θυγάτηρ, τρός, ἡ** a daughter; hence (Hebraistic?), of any female descendent, however far removed; even of one unrelated: my young lady.

## θυγάτριον
**θυγάτριον, ου, τό** a little (young) daughter.

## θύελλα
**θύελλα, ης, ἡ** a storm, tempest, whirlwind.

## θύϊνος
**θύϊνος, η, ον** of the sandarach (so-called citron) tree.

## θυμίαμα
**θυμίαμα, ατος, τό** incense.

## θυμιατήριον
**θυμιατήριον, ου, τό** (ordinarily: censer, but) either the altar of incense, or the shovel, on which the high-priest poured the coals, when he entered the Holy of Holies on the Day of Atonement.

## θυμιάω
**θυμιάω** I burn incense.

## θυμομαχέω
**θυμομαχέω** (lit: I fight desperately, hence) I am furiously angry with.

## θυμόομαι
**θυμόω** I provoke to anger; pass: I am wroth or very angry.

## θυμός
**θυμός, οῦ, ὁ** an outburst of passion, wrath.

## θύρα
**θύρα, ας, ἡ** (a) a door, (b) met: an opportunity.

## θυρεός
**θυρεός, οῦ, ὁ** the heavy oblong Roman shield.

## θυρίς
**θυρίς, ίδος, ἡ** a small opening, window.

## θυρωρός
**θυρωρός, οῦ, ὁ, ἡ** a door-keeper, porter.

## θυσία
**θυσία, ας, ἡ** abstr. and concr: sacrifice; a sacrifice, offering.

## θυσιαστήριον
**θυσιαστήριον, ου, τό** an altar (for sacrifice).

## θύω
**θύω** I sacrifice, generally an animal; hence: I kill.

## Θωμᾶς
**Θωμᾶς, ᾶ, ὁ** Thomas, also called Didymus, one of the Twelve.

## θώραξ
**θώραξ, ακός, ὁ** a breast-plate, corslet, cuirass.

## Ἰάϊρος
**Ἰάειρος, ου, ὁ** Jairus, a Jewish ruler of the synagogue.

## Ἰακώβ
**Ἰακώβ, ὁ** (Hebrew), Jacob, (a) the patriarch, son of Isaac, (b) father of Joseph, the husband of Mary.

## Ἰάκωβος
**Ἰάκωβος, ου, ὁ** James, (a) the Small, son of Alphaeus, and one of the Twelve, (b) half-brother of Jesus, (c) father (?) of Jude, (d) son of Zebedee, and brother of John, one of the Twelve, killed A.D. 44.

## ἴαμα
**ἴαμα, ατος, τό** healing, curing, remedy.

## Ἰαμβρῆς
**Ἰαμβρῆς, ου, ὁ** Jambres, a sorcerer at the court of the Pharaoh.

## Ἰανναί
**Ἰαννά, ὁ** Jannai, an ancestor of Jesus; Jannai was the son of Joseph, and father of Melchi.

## Ἰάννης
**Ἰαννῆς, ου, ὁ** Jannes, a sorcerer at the court of the Pharaoh.

## ἰάομαι
**ἰάομαι** I heal, generally of the physical, sometimes of spiritual, disease.

## Ἰάρετ
**Ἰαρέδ, ὁ** (Hebrew), Jared, son of Mahalalel and father of Enoch.

## ἴασις
**ἴασις, εως, ἡ** a cure, healing.

## ἴασπις
**ἴασπις, ιδος, ἡ** jasper; a precious stone.

## Ἰάσων
**Ἰάσων, ονος, ὁ** Jason, a Christian of Thessalonica, perhaps the same as the "relative" of Paul.

## ἰατρός
**ἰατρός, οῦ, ὁ** a physician.

## ἴδε
**ἴδε** See! Lo! Behold! Look!.

## ἴδιος
**ἴδιος, α, ν** one's own, belonging to one, private, personal; one's own people, one's own family, home, property.

## ἰδιώτης
**ἰδιώτης, ου, ὁ** (unofficial, hence) an amateur, an unprofessional man, a layman; an ungifted person.

## ἰδού
**ἰδού** See! Lo! Behold! Look!.

## Ἰδουμαία
**Ἰδουμαία, ας, ἡ** Idumea, Edom, a district of Arabia, immediately south of Judea.

## ἱδρώς
**ἱδρώς, ῶτος, ὁ** sweat, perspiration.

## Ἰεζάβελ
**Ἰεζάβελ, ἡ** Jezebel, name given to a false prophetess of Thyatira, possibly borrowed from the name of Ahab's wife, queen of Israel.

## Ἱεράπολις
**Ἱεράπολις, εως, ἡ** Hierapolis, a city of the Lycus valley in Phrygia, near Laodicea and Colossae.

## ἱερατεία
**ἱερατεία, ας, ἡ** the duty (office) of a priest, priesthood.

## ἱεράτευμα
**ἱεράτευμα, ατος, τό** the act or office of priesthood.

## ἱερατεύω
**ἱερατεύω** I serve as priest.

## Ἰερεμίας
**Ἰερεμίας, ου, ὁ** Jeremiah, Hebrew prophet.

## ἱερεύς
**ἱερεύς, έως, ὁ** a priest, one who offers sacrifice to a god (in Jewish and pagan religions; of Christians only met.).

## Ἰεριχώ
**Ἰεριχώ, ἡ** Jericho, a city a little north of the Dead Sea.

## ἱερόθυτος
**εἰδωλόθυτος, ον** (of meat), sacrificed to an image (or an idol).

## ἱερόν
**ἱερόν, οῦ, τό** a temple, either the whole building, or specifically the outer courts, open to worshippers.

## ἱεροπρεπής
**ἱεροπρεπής, ές** suitable to a sacred character, reverent.

## ἱερός
**ἱερός, ά, όν** sacred, holy, set apart.

## Ἱεροσόλυμα
**Ἱεροσόλυμα, ατος, τά, ἡ** the Greek form of the Hebrew name: Jerusalem.

## Ἱεροσολυμίτης
**Ἱεροσολυμίτης, ου, ὁ** an inhabitant of Jerusalem.

## ἱεροσυλέω
**ἱεροσυλέω** I commit sacrilege, rob a temple.

## ἱερόσυλος
**ἱερόσυλος, ον** robbing temples, but possibly simply: sacrilegious.

## ἱερουργέω
**ἱερουργέω** I minister in holy things.

## Ἰερουσαλήμ
**Ἱερουσαλήμ, ἡ** (Aramaic form), Jerusalem, the capital of Palestine: hence Judaism, and allegorically, Christendom, the Christian Church.

## ἱερωσύνη
**ἱερωσύνη, ης, ἡ** the abstract notion of the priestly office.

## Ἰεσσαί
**Ἰεσσαί, ὁ** (Hebrew), Jesse, son of Obed, and father of King David.

## Ἰεφθάε
**Ἰεφθάε, ὁ** (Hebrew), Jephthah, one of the Judges of Israel.

## Ἰεχονίας
**Ἰεχονίας, ου, ὁ** (Hebrew), Jechoniah, son of Josiah and father of Salathiel.

## Ἰησοῦς
**Ἰησοῦς, οῦ, ὁ** Jesus; the Greek form of Joshua; Jesus, son of Eliezer; Jesus, surnamed Justus.

## ἱκανός
**ἱκανός, ή, όν** (a) considerable, sufficient, of number, quantity, time, (b) of persons: sufficiently strong (good, etc.), worthy, suitable, with various constructions, (c) many, much.

## ἱκανότης
**ἱκανότης, ητος, ἡ** sufficiency, ability, power, fitness.

## ἱκανόω
**ἱκανόω** I make sufficient, render fit, qualify.

## ἱκετηρία
**ἱκετηρία, ας, ἡ** (originally: the olive branch held in the hand of the suppliant), supplication, entreaty.

## ἰκμάς
**ἰκμάς, άδος, ἡ** moisture.

## Ἰκόνιον
**Ἰκόνιον, ου, τό** Iconium, a Phrygian city of the Roman province Galatia (modern Konia).

## ἱλαρός
**ἱλαρός, ά, όν** joyous, cheerful, not grudging.

## ἱλαρότης
**ἱλαρότης, ητος, ἡ** cheerfulness, graciousness.

## ἱλάσκομαι
**ἱλάσκομαι** (a) I have mercy on, show favor to, (b) trans. with object of sins: I forgive, pardon.

## ἱλασμός
**ἱλασμός, οῦ, ὁ** a propitiation (of an angry god), atoning sacrifice.

## ἱλαστήριον
**ἱλαστήριον, ου, τό** (a) a sin offering, by which the wrath of the deity shall be appeased; a means of propitiation, (b) the covering of the ark, which was sprinkled with the atoning blood on the Day of Atonement.

## ἵλεως
**ἵλεως** propitious, forgiving, merciful.

## Ἰλλυρικόν
**Ἰλλυρικόν, οῦ, τό** Illyricum, a Roman province, afterwards called Dalmatia, bounded by Pannonia on the north, Macedonia on the south, Moesia on the east, and the Adriatic Sea on the west.

## ἱμάς
**ἱμάς, άντος, ὁ** a thong, strap, (a) for binding a man who is to be flogged, (b) for fastening a sandal or shoe.

## ἱματίζω
**ἱματίζω** I clothe, provide clothing for; pass: I am clothed.

## ἱμάτιον
**ἱμάτιον, ου, τό** a long flowing outer garment, tunic.

## ἱματισμός
**ἱματισμός, οῦ, ὁ** a collective word: raiment, clothing.

## ἵνα
**ἵνα** in order that, so that.

## ἱνατί
**ἱνατί** why? for what reason?

## Ἰόππη
**Ἰόππη, ης, ἡ** Joppa, a coast town of Judea, west-north-west of Jerusalem.

## Ἰορδάνης
**Ἰορδάνης, ου, ὁ** the Jordan, a great river flowing due south and bounding Galilee, Samaria, and Judea on the east.

## ἰός
**ἰός, οῦ, ὁ** poison, rust; an arrow.

## Ἰουδαία
**Ἰουδαία, ας, ἡ** Judea, a Roman province, capital Jerusalem.

## ἰουδαΐζω
**Ἰουδαΐζω** I live as a Jew (in religion, ceremonially).

## Ἰουδαϊκός
**Ἰουδαϊκός, ή, όν** Jewish, Judaic.

## Ἰουδαϊκῶς
**Ἰουδαϊκῶς** in the manner of Jews (religiously, ceremonially).

## Ἰουδαῖος
**Ἰουδαῖος, αία, αῖον** Jewish.

## Ἰουδαϊσμός
**Ἰουδαϊσμός, οῦ, ὁ** the Jewish religion, Judaism.

## Ἰούδας
**Ἰούδας, α, ὁ** Judah, Judas, Jude.

## Ἰουλία
**Ἰουλία, ας, ἡ** Julia, a Roman Christian, probably a slave or freed from the Imperial household.

## Ἰούλιος
**Ἰούλιος, ου, ὁ** Julius, a Roman centurion on special service.

## Ἰουνία
**Ἰουνιᾶς, ᾶ, ὁ** Junia, Junias, a Roman Christian.

## Ἰοῦστος
**Ἰοῦστος, ου, ὁ** Justus, (a) a surname of Joseph Barsabbas, one of the two nominated to fill Judas' place as apostle, (b) Titius Justus, a Corinthian Christian, (c) surname of Jesus, a Christian with Paul in Rome.

## ἱππεύς
**ἱππεύς, έως, ὁ** a horse-soldier, a mounted soldier, a cavalryman.

## ἱππικόν
**ἱππικόν** cavalry, horsemen

## ἵππος
**ἵππος, ου, ὁ** a horse.

## ἶρις
**ἶρις, ιδος, ἡ** a rainbow or halo.

## Ἰσαάκ
**Ἰσαάκ, ὁ** (Hebrew), Isaac, the patriarch.

## ἰσάγγελος
**ἰσάγγελος, ον** equal to or like the angels.

## Ἰσκαριώθ
**Ἰσκαριώτης, ου, ὁ** Iscariot, surname of Judas.

## Ἰσκαριώτης
**Ἰσκαριώτης, ου, ὁ** Iscariot, surname of Judas.

## ἴσος
**ἴσος, η, ον** equal, equivalent, identical.

## ἰσότης
**ἰσότης, ητος, ἡ** equality; equality of treatment, fairness.

## ἰσότιμος
**ἰσότιμος, ον** equally privileged, equal in honor.

## ἰσόψυχος
**ἰσόψυχος, ον** like-minded, of the same mind or spirit.

## Ἰσραήλ
**Ἰσραήλ, ὁ** (Hebrew), Israel, surname of Jacob, then the Jewish people, the people of God.

## Ἰσραηλίτης
**Ἰσραηλίτης, ου, ὁ** an Israelite, one of the chosen people of Israel, a Jew.

## Ἰσσαχάρ
**Ἰσαχάρ, ὁ** Issachar, a proper name.

## ἵστημι
**ἵστημι** trans: (a) I make to stand, place, set up, establish, appoint; mid: I place myself, stand, (b) I set in balance, weigh; intrans: (c) I stand, stand by, stand still; met: I stand ready, stand firm, am steadfast.

## ἱστορέω
**ἱστορέω** I get acquainted with, visit.

## ἰσχυρός
**ἰσχυρός, ά, όν** strong (originally and generally of physical strength); mighty, powerful, vehement, sure.

## ἰσχύς
**ἰσχύς, ύος, ἡ** strength (absolutely), power, might, force, ability.

## ἰσχύω
**ἰσχύω** I have strength, am strong, am in full health and vigor, am able; meton: I prevail.

## ἴσως
**ἴσως** perhaps, equally; it may be that.

## Ἰταλία
**Ἰταλία, ας, ἡ** Italy.

## Ἰταλικός
**Ἰταλικός, ή, όν** Italian.

## Ἰτουραῖος
**Ἰτουραῖος, α, ον** Ituraean, an adjective applied to a district also called Trachonitis, about 60 miles east of the Sea of Galilee, and partly inhabited by the nomad tribe called Ituraeans.

## ἰχθύδιον
**ἰχθύδιον, ου, τό** a small fish.

## ἰχθύς
**ἰχθύς, ύος, ὁ** a fish.

## ἴχνος
**ἴχνος, ους, τό** a track, footstep.

## Ἰωαθάμ
**Ἰωάθαμ, ὁ** Jotham, son of Uzziah and father of Ahaz.

## Ἰωανάν
**Ἰωανάν, ὁ** Joanan, a proper name.

## Ἰωάννα
**Ἰωάννα, ας, ἡ** (Hebrew), Joanna, Johanna, wife of Chuza, Herod's steward.

## Ἰωάννης
**Ἰωάννης, ου, ὁ** John: the Baptist, the apostle, a member of the Sanhedrin, or John Mark.

## Ἰώβ
**Ἰώβ, ὁ** (Hebrew), Job, the hero of the book of that name in the Old Testament.

## Ἰωβήδ
**Ὠβήδ, ὁ** Obed.

## Ἰωβήλ
**Ὠβήδ, ὁ** Obed.

## Ἰωδά
**Ἰούδας, α, ὁ** Judah, Judas, Jude.

## Ἰωήλ
**Ἰωήλ, ὁ** Joel, the Hebrew prophet.

## Ἰωνάμ
**Ἰωνάν, ὁ** (Hebrew), Jonam, an ancestor of Jesus.

## Ἰωνᾶς
**Ἰωνᾶς, ᾶ, ὁ** (Hebrew), Jonah, the Hebrew prophet.

## Ἰωράμ
**Ἰωράμ, ὁ** (Hebrew), Joram, Jehoram, son of Jehoshaphat and father of Uzziah.

## Ἰωρίμ
**Ἰωρείμ, ὁ** (Hebrew), Jorim, an ancestor of Jesus.

## Ἰωσαφάτ
**Ἰωσαφάτ, ὁ** (Hebrew), Jehoshaphat, king of Judah, son of Asaph, father of Joram, an ancestor of Jesus.

## Ἰωσῆς
**Ἰωσῆς, ῆτος, ὁ** (Hebrew), Joses (a) son of Eliezer, (b) son of Mary, half-brother of Jesus, (c) surnamed Barnabas (also called Joseph).

## Ἰωσήφ
**Ἰωσήφ, ὁ** Joseph, a proper name.

## Ἰωσήχ
**Ἰωσήφ, ὁ** Joseph, a proper name.

## Ἰωσίας
**Ἰωσίας, ου, ὁ** (Hebrew), Josiah, king of Judah.

## ἰῶτα
**ἰῶτα, τό** iota, a small letter of the Greek alphabet, used in the NT (like yod, the Hebrew or rather Aramaic letter which was the smallest of all) to indicate the smallest part.

## κἀγώ
**κἀγώ** I also, I too, but I.

## καθά
**καθά** just as.

## καθαίρεσις
**καθαίρεσις, εως, ἡ** taking down, razing, destroying.

## καθαιρέω
**καθαιρέω** (a) I take down, pull down, depose, destroy.

## καθαίρω
**καθαίρω** I cleanse, purify, prune.

## καθάπερ
**καθάπερ** even as, just as.

## καθάπτω
**καθάπτω** I lay hold of, fasten on to, seize, attack.

## καθαρίζω
**καθαρίζω** I cleanse, make clean, literally, ceremonially, or spiritually, according to context.

## καθαρισμός
**καθαρισμός, οῦ, ὁ** cleansing, purifying, purification, literal, ceremonial, or moral; met: expiation.

## καθαρός
**καθαρός, ά, όν** clean, pure, unstained, either literally or ceremonially or spiritually; guiltless, innocent, upright.

## καθαρότης
**καθαρότης, ητος, ἡ** cleanness, purity.

## καθέδρα
**καθέδρα, ας, ἡ** a seat, chair.

## καθέζομαι
**καθέζομαι** I am sitting, sit down, am seated.

## καθεξῆς
**καθεξῆς** in order, in succession, in the time immediately after, just after.

## καθεύδω
**καθεύδω** I sleep, am sleeping.

## καθηγητής
**καθηγητής, ου, ὁ** a leader, teacher, guide, master.

## καθήκω
**καθήκω** I come down, come to; I am unfit, proper.

## κάθημαι
**κάθημαι** I sit, am seated, enthroned; I dwell, reside.

## καθημερινός
**καθημερινός, ή, όν** daily, day-by-day.

## καθίζω
**καθίζω** (a) trans: I make to sit; I set, appoint, (b) intrans: I sit down, am seated, stay.

## καθίημι
**καθίημι** I send, let down, lower.

## καθίστημι
**καθίστημι** I set down, bring down to a place; I set in order, appoint, make, constitute.

## καθό
**καθό** according to which thing, as, according as.

## καθόλου
**καθόλου** one the whole, in general, altogether.

## καθοπλίζω
**καθοπλίζω** I arm completely.

## καθοράω
**καθοράω** I see clearly, perceive, discern.

## καθότι
**καθότι** (a) in proportion as, according as, (b) because.

## καθώς
**καθώς** according to the manner in which, in the degree that, just as, as.

## καθώσπερ
**καθάπερ** even as, just as.

## καί
**καί** and, even, also, namely.

## Καϊάφας
**Καϊάφας, ᾶ, ὁ** Caiaphas, Jewish high priest.

## Κάϊν
**Κάϊν, ὁ** (Hebrew), Cain, son of Adam and Eve and brother of Abel.

## Καϊνάμ
**Καϊνάν, ὁ** (Hebrew), Cainan, one of the ancestors of Jesus.

## καινός
**καινός, ή, όν** fresh, new, unused, novel.

## καινότης
**καινότης, ητος, ἡ** freshness, newness, novelty.

## καίπερ
**καίπερ** although, though.

## καιρός
**καιρός, οῦ, ὁ** fitting season, season, opportunity, occasion, time.

## Καῖσαρ
**Καῖσαρ, αρος, ὁ** Caesar, a surname of the gens Iulia, which became practically synonymous with the Emperor for the time being; in the Gospels it always refers to Tiberias.

## Καισάρεια
**Καισάρεια, ας, ἡ** Two cities of Palestine: one in Galilee (Caesarea Philippi), the other on the coast of the Mediterranean.

## καίτοι
**καίτοι** and yet, although, though.

## καίτοιγε
**καίτοιγε** and yet, although, indeed.

## καίω
**καίω** I ignite, light, burn, lit. and met; I consume with fire.

## κἀκεῖ
**κἀκεῖ** and there, and yonder, there also.

## κἀκεῖθεν
**κἀκεῖθεν** and thence, and from there; and then afterwards.

## κἀκεῖνος
**κἀκεῖνος, η, ο** and he, she, it, and that.

## κακία
**κακία, ας, ἡ** (a) evil (i.e. trouble, labor, misfortune), (b) wickedness, (c) vicious disposition, malice, spite.

## κακοήθεια
**κακοήθεια, ας, ἡ** evil-mindedness, malignity, malevolence.

## κακολογέω
**κακολογέω** I speak evil of, curse, revile, abuse.

## κακοπαθέω
**κακοπαθέω** I suffer evil, endure affliction.

## κακοπαθία
**κακοπάθεια, ας, ἡ** experience of evil, suffering, distress, affliction, perseverance.

## κακοποιέω
**κακοποιέω** I do harm, do wrong, do evil, commit sin.

## κακοποιός
**κακοποιός, όν** doing evil; subst: an evil-doer.

## κακός
**κακός, ή, όν** bad, evil, in the widest sense.

## κακοῦργος
**κακοῦργος, ον** (lit: an evil-worker), a criminal.

## κακουχέομαι
**κακουχέω** I treat evilly, hurt, torment.

## κακόω
**κακόω** I treat badly, afflict, embitter, make angry.

## κακῶς
**κακῶς** badly, evilly, wrongly.

## κάκωσις
**κάκωσις, εως, ἡ** affliction, ill-treatment, oppression, misery.

## καλάμη
**καλάμη, ῆς, ἡ** stubble, straw, the stalk.

## κάλαμος
**κάλαμος, ου, ὁ** a reed; a reed-pen, reed-staff, measuring rod.

## καλέω
**καλέω** (a) I call, summon, invite, (b) I call, name.

## καλλιέλαιος
**καλλιέλαιος, οῦ, ἡ** a cultivated olive tree.

## καλοδιδάσκαλος
**καλοδιδάσκαλος, ου, ὁ, ἡ** a teacher of that which is noble (honorable) and good.

## καλοποιέω
**καλοποιέω** I do well, act honorably, do what is right.

## Καλός
**Καλός** Fair (Καλοὶ Λιμένες, Fair Havens, a harbour of Crete)

## καλός
**καλός, ή, όν** beautiful, as an outward sign of the inward good, noble, honorable character; good, worthy, honorable, noble, and seen to be so.

## κάλυμμα
**κάλυμμα, ατος, τό** a covering, especially a covering of head and face, a veil.

## καλύπτω
**καλύπτω** I veil, hide, conceal, envelop.

## καλῶς
**καλῶς** well, nobly, honorably, rightly.

## κάμηλος
**κάμηλος, ου, ὁ, ἡ** a camel or dromedary.

## κάμινος
**κάμινος, ου, ἡ** a furnace, oven, kiln.

## καμμύω
**καμμύω** I close, shut the eyes.

## κάμνω
**κάμνω** I work, am weary, am sick.

## κάμπτω
**κάμπτω** I bend, bow.

## κἄν
**κἄν** and if, even if, even, at least.

## Κανά
**Κανᾶ, ἡ** Cana, a town in Galilee.

## Καναναῖος
**Κανανίτης, ου, ὁ** a Canaanite.

## Κανδάκη
**Κανδάκη, ης, ἡ** Candace, a proper name; the Candace, a dynastic name for queens of the Ethiopians in Abyssinia.

## κανών
**κανών, όνος, ὁ** (lit: a level, ruler), a rule, regulation, rule of conduct or doctrine, (b) a measured (defined) area, province.

## καπηλεύω
**καπηλεύω** I hawk, trade in, deal in for purposes of gain.

## καπνός
**καπνός, οῦ, ὁ** smoke.

## Καππαδοκία
**Καππαδοκία, ας, ἡ** Cappadocia, a large Roman province in the central eastern part of Asia Minor.

## καρδία
**καρδία, ας, ἡ** lit: the heart; mind, character, inner self, will, intention, center.

## καρδιογνώστης
**καρδιογνώστης, ου, ὁ** a knower of the inner life (character, hearts).

## Κάρπος
**Κάρπος, ου, ὁ** Carpus, a Christian of Troas.

## καρπός
**καρπός, οῦ, ὁ** (a) fruit, generally vegetable, sometimes animal, (b) met: fruit, deed, action, result, (c) profit, gain.

## καρποφορέω
**καρποφορέω** I bear fruit.

## καρποφόρος
**καρποφόρος, ον** fruitful.

## καρτερέω
**καρτερέω** I persevere, endure, am steadfast, patient.

## κάρφος
**κάρφος, ους, τό** a dry stalk, chip of wood, twig, splinter, chaff.

## κατά
**κατά** gen: against, down from, throughout, by; acc: over against, among, daily, day-by-day, each day, according to, by way of.

## καταβαίνω
**καταβαίνω** I go down, come down, either from the sky or from higher land, descend.

## καταβάλλω
**καταβάλλω** (a) mid: I lay, of a foundation, (b) met: I cast down, prostrate.

## καταβαρέω
**καταβαρέω** I burden, oppress, weigh down.

## καταβαρύνομαι
**καταβαρύνομαι** to be weighed down, be heavy

## κατάβασις
**κατάβασις, εως, ἡ** descent.

## καταβολή
**καταβολή, ῆς, ἡ** (a) foundation, (b) depositing, sowing, deposit, technically used of the act of conception.

## καταβραβεύω
**καταβραβεύω** of the umpire in a contest: I decide against, take part against, condemn (perhaps with the idea of assumption, officialism).

## καταγγελεύς
**καταγγελεύς, έως, ὁ** a reporter, announcer, proclaimer, herald.

## καταγγέλλω
**καταγγέλλω** I declare openly, proclaim, preach, laud, celebrate.

## καταγελάω
**καταγελάω** I laugh at, ridicule.

## καταγινώσκω
**καταγινώσκω** I condemn, blame.

## κατάγνυμι
**κατάγνυμι** I break down (in pieces), crush, break into.

## κατάγω
**κατάγω** I lead down, bring down, either from a high place on land or to a lower (or actually to the sea-coast), or from the high seas to land.

## καταγωνίζομαι
**καταγωνίζομαι** I subdue (in warfare); I struggle against, conquer, overcome.

## καταδέω
**καταδέω** I bind up, bandage.

## κατάδηλος
**κατάδηλος, ον** quite clear, evident.

## καταδικάζω
**καταδικάζω** I condemn, pass sentence upon.

## καταδίκη
**δίκη, ης, ἡ** (a) (originally: custom, usage) right, justice, (b) process of law, judicial hearing, (c) execution of sentence, punishment, penalty, (d) justice, vengeance.

## καταδιώκω
**καταδιώκω** I hunt down, follow closely.

## καταδουλόω
**καταδουλόω** I enslave.

## καταδυναστεύω
**καταδυναστεύω** I overpower, quell, treat harshly.

## κατάθεμα
**κατάθεμα, ατος, τό** a curse, an accursed thing.

## καταθεματίζω
**καταθεματίζω** I curse, devote to destruction.

## καταισχύνω
**καταισχύνω** I shame, disgrace, bring to shame, put to utter confusion, frustrate.

## κατακαίω
**κατακαίω** I burn up, consume entirely.

## κατακαλύπτομαι
**κατακαλύπτω** I veil, cover the head.

## κατακαυχάομαι
**κατακαυχάομαι** I boast against, exult over.

## κατάκειμαι
**κατάκειμαι** I recline (at table); more often: I keep my bed, am lying ill (in bed).

## κατακλάω
**κατακλάω** I break in pieces, break up.

## κατακλείω
**κατακλείω** I shut up, confine.

## κατακληρονομέω
**κατακληρονομέω** I give as an inheritance, distribute by lot.

## κατακλίνω
**κατακλίνω** I cause to recline at table; mid. and pass: I recline at table.

## κατακλύζω
**κατακλύζω** I flood over, overwhelm, inundate.

## κατακλυσμός
**κατακλυσμός, οῦ, ὁ** a deluge, flood.

## κατακολουθέω
**κατακολουθέω** I follow after.

## κατακόπτω
**κατακόπτω** I cut up, cut in pieces, mangle, wound.

## κατακρημνίζω
**κατακρημνίζω** I cast down headlong, throw over a precipice.

## κατάκριμα
**κατάκριμα, ατος, τό** punishment following condemnation, penal servitude, penalty.

## κατακρίνω
**κατακρίνω** I condemn, judge worthy of punishment.

## κατάκρισις
**κατάκρισις, εως, ἡ** condemnation, censure.

## κατακυριεύω
**κατακυριεύω** I exercise authority over, overpower, master.

## καταλαλέω
**καταλαλέω** I speak evil of, rail at, slander.

## καταλαλιά
**καταλαλιά, ας, ἡ** evil-speaking, backbiting, detraction, slander.

## κατάλαλος
**κατάλαλος, ον** slanderous, back-biting; subst: a railer, defamer.

## καταλαμβάνω
**καταλαμβάνω** (a) I seize tight hold of, arrest, catch, capture, appropriate, (b) I overtake, (c) mid. aor: I perceived, comprehended.

## καταλέγω
**καταλέγω** I enter in a list, register, enroll.

## καταλείπω
**καταλείπω** I leave behind, desert, abandon, forsake; I leave remaining, reserve.

## καταλιθάζω
**καταλιθάζω** I stone down, stone to death, overwhelm with stones.

## καταλλαγή
**καταλλαγή, ῆς, ἡ** reconciliation, restoration to favor.

## καταλλάσσω
**καταλλάσσω** I change, exchange, reconcile.

## κατάλοιπος
**κατάλοιπος, ον** left behind; the rest, the remainder.

## κατάλυμα
**κατάλυμα, ατος, τό** an inn, lodging-place.

## καταλύω
**καταλύω** (lit: I loosen thoroughly), (a) trans: I break up, overthrow, destroy, both lit. and met., (b) I unyoke, unharness a carriage horse or pack animal; hence: I put up, lodge, find a lodging.

## καταμανθάνω
**καταμανθάνω** I understand, take in a fact about, consider carefully.

## καταμαρτυρέω
**καταμαρτυρέω** I give evidence (bear witness) against.

## καταμένω
**καταμένω** I wait, stay (with), remain, abide, dwell.

## καταναλίσκω
**καταναλίσκω** I use up, spend, consume (as with fire).

## καταναρκάω
**καταναρκάω** (properly a medical term: I stupefy, hence) I burden, encumber.

## κατανεύω
**κατανεύω** I nod, make a sign, beckon.

## κατανοέω
**κατανοέω** I take note of, perceive, consider carefully, discern, detect, make account of.

## καταντάω
**καταντάω** (a) I come down, either from high land to lower (or actually to the sea-coast), or from the high seas to the coast; hence met: I arrive at, reach (my destination), (b) of property: I come down (descend) by inheritance to an heir.

## κατάνυξις
**κατάνυξις, εως, ἡ** deep sleep, torpor, insensibility, stupor.

## κατανύσσομαι
**κατανύσσομαι** met: I am pierced, stung, smitten.

## καταξιόω
**καταξιόω** I deem (count) worthy.

## καταπατέω
**καταπατέω** I trample down, trample under foot (lit. and met.), spurn.

## κατάπαυσις
**κατάπαυσις, εως, ἡ** (in the Old Testament of the rest attained by the settlement in Canaan), resting, rest, dwelling, habitation.

## καταπαύω
**καταπαύω** (a) trans: I cause to rest, bring to rest; I cause to refrain, (b) intrans: I rest.

## καταπέτασμα
**καταπέτασμα, ατος, τό** (lit: that which is spread out downwards, that which hangs down), a curtain, veil, of that which separated the Holy of Holies from the outer parts of the temple at Jerusalem, also of an outer curtain at the entrance to the Holy Place in the same temple.

## καταπίνω
**καταπίνω** I drink down, swallow, devour, destroy, consume.

## καταπίπτω
**καταπίπτω** I fall down, fall prostrate.

## καταπλέω
**καταπλέω** I sail down (from the high seas to the shore).

## καταπονέω
**καταπονέω** I exhaust by labor or suffering, wear out, overpower, oppress.

## καταποντίζομαι
**καταποντίζω** I sink in the sea, pass: I drown, am submerged.

## κατάρα
**κατάρα, ας, ἡ** cursing; a curse; meton: a doomed one.

## καταράομαι
**καταράομαι** I curse.

## καταργέω
**καταργέω** (a) I make idle (inactive), make of no effect, annul, abolish, bring to naught, (b) I discharge, sever, separate from.

## καταριθμέω
**καταριθμέω** I number among, count with.

## καταρτίζω
**καταρτίζω** (a) I fit (join) together; met: I compact together, (b) act. and mid: I prepare, perfect, for his (its) full destination or use, bring into its proper condition (whether for the first time, or after a lapse).

## κατάρτισις
**κατάρτισις, εως, ἡ** a perfecting, making fit.

## καταρτισμός
**καταρτισμός, ου, ὁ** a bringing to a condition of fitness, perfecting.

## κατασείω
**κατασείω** I shake (the hand) up and down, wave; intrans: I beckon for silence.

## κατασκάπτω
**κατασκάπτω** I dig down under, demolish, undermine.

## κατασκευάζω
**κατασκευάζω** I build, construct, prepare, make ready.

## κατασκηνόω
**κατασκηνόω** I encamp, take up my quarters, tabernacle, pitch my tent, dwell.

## κατασκήνωσις
**κατασκήνωσις, εως, ἡ** a dwelling-place.

## κατασκιάζω
**κατασκιάζω** I overshadow.

## κατασκοπέω
**κατασκοπέω** I view closely, inspect, spy out.

## κατάσκοπος
**κατάσκοπος, ου, ὁ** a spy, scout.

## κατασοφίζομαι
**κατασοφίζομαι** I deal craftily with, outwit.

## καταστέλλω
**καταστέλλω** I let down, lower; I keep down, restrain, pacify.

## κατάστημα
**κατάστημα, ατος, τό** behavior, conduct, deportment, demeanor.

## καταστολή
**καταστολή, ῆς, ἡ** garb, clothing, dress, attire.

## καταστρέφω
**καταστρέφω** I overturn, overthrow.

## καταστρηνιάω
**καταστρηνιάω** I grow wanton towards.

## καταστροφή
**καταστροφή, ῆς, ἡ** overthrow, destruction, material or spiritual.

## καταστρώννυμι
**καταστρώννυμι** I strew or spread over; I lay low, overthrow.

## κατασύρω
**κατασύρω** I pull down, drag away.

## κατασφάζω
**κατασφάζω** I slaughter, kill off, slay.

## κατασφραγίζω
**κατασφραγίζω** I seal up, secure with a seal.

## κατάσχεσις
**κατάσχεσις, εως, ἡ** a possession, holding fast.

## κατατίθημι
**κατατίθημι** (a) I lay down, deposit, (b) mid: I lay down or deposit a favor, with the view of receiving one in return, seek favor.

## κατατομή
**κατατομή, ῆς, ἡ** a mutilation, spoiling.

## κατατρέχω
**κατατρέχω** I run down.

## καταφέρω
**καταφέρω** I bear down, overpower; I give a vote or verdict, bring charges.

## καταφεύγω
**καταφεύγω** I flee for refuge (implying that the refuge is reached); aor. indicates moment of arrival.

## καταφθείρω
**καταφθείρω** I destroy, corrupt.

## καταφιλέω
**καταφιλέω** I kiss affectionately.

## καταφρονέω
**καταφρονέω** I despise, scorn, and show it by active insult, disregard.

## καταφρονητής
**καταφρονητής, οῦ, ὁ** a despiser, scorner.

## καταχέω
**καταχέω** I pour (down) upon.

## καταχθόνιος
**καταχθόνιος, ον** under the earth, subterranean, infernal.

## καταχράομαι
**καταχράομαι** I use to the full, use up.

## καταψύχω
**καταψύχω** I cool, refresh.

## κατείδωλος
**κατείδωλος, ον** full of images of idols, grossly idolatrous.

## κατέναντι
**κατέναντι** opposite, in front (of), over against.

## κατενώπιον
**κατενώπιον** before the face of, over against.

## κατεξουσιάζω
**κατεξουσιάζω** I have (exercise) power (authority) over.

## κατεργάζομαι
**κατεργάζομαι** I effect by labor, achieve, work out, bring about.

## κατέρχομαι
**κατέρχομαι** I come down from sky to earth, or from high land to lower land (or to the coast), or from the high seas to the shore.

## κατεσθίω
**κατεσθίω** I eat up, eat till it is finished, devour, squander, annoy, injure.

## κατευθύνω
**κατευθύνω** (a) I make straight, (b) met: I put in the right way, direct.

## κατευλογέω
**εὐλογέω** (lit: I speak well of) I bless; pass: I am blessed.

## κατεφίστημι
**κατεφίστημι** I rush, assault, rise up against.

## κατέχω
**κατέχω** (a) I hold fast, bind, arrest, (b) I take possession of, lay hold of, (c) I hold back, detain, restrain, (d) I hold a ship, keep its head.

## κατηγορέω
**κατηγορέω** I accuse, charge, prosecute.

## κατηγορία
**κατηγορία, ας, ἡ** an accusation, charge.

## κατήγορος
**κατήγορος, ου, ὁ** an accuser, prosecutor.

## κατήγωρ
**κατήγορος, ου, ὁ** an accuser, prosecutor.

## κατήφεια
**κατήφεια, ας, ἡ** a downcast countenance as a sign of sorrow, gloominess, gloom, dejection.

## κατηχέω
**κατηχέω** I instruct orally, teach, inform.

## κατιόομαι
**κατιόω** I rust; pass: I am rusted, tarnished.

## κατισχύω
**κατισχύω** I prevail against, overpower, get the upper hand.

## κατοικέω
**κατοικέω** I dwell in, settle in, am established in (permanently), inhabit.

## κατοίκησις
**κατοίκησις, εως, ἡ** a dwelling, abode, habitation.

## κατοικητήριον
**κατοικητήριον, ου, τό** a habitation, dwelling-place, abode.

## κατοικία
**κατοικία, ας, ἡ** a dwelling, habitation, settlement.

## κατοικίζω
**κατοικέω** I dwell in, settle in, am established in (permanently), inhabit.

## κατοπτρίζομαι
**κατοπτρίζομαι** I mirror, reflect.

## κάτω
**κάτω** (a) down, below, also: downwards, (b) lower, under, less, of a length of time.

## κατώτερος
**κατώτερος, α, ον** lower.

## κατωτέρω
**κάτω** (a) down, below, also: downwards, (b) lower, under, less, of a length of time.

## Καῦδα
**Κλαῦδη, ης, ἡ** Cauda, Clauda, an island twenty-three miles south of the western end of Crete.

## καῦμα
**καῦμα, ατος, τό** burning heat, heat.

## καυματίζω
**καυματίζω** I burn, scorch.

## καῦσις
**καῦσις, εως, ἡ** a burning up, being burned.

## καυσόομαι
**καυσόω** I burn with great heat.

## καυστηριάζω
**καυτηριάζω** I cauterize, burn with a hot iron; hence met: I sear.

## καύσων
**καύσων, ῶνος, ὁ** a scorching heat, hot wind.

## καυχάομαι
**καυχάομαι** I boast; I glory (exult) proudly.

## καύχημα
**καύχημα, ατος, τό** a boasting; a ground of boasting (glorying, exultation).

## καύχησις
**καύχησις, εως, ἡ** the act of boasting, glorying, exultation.

## Καφαρναούμ
**Καπερναούμ, ἡ** Capernaum, a town of Galilee.

## Κεγχρεαί
**Κεγχρεαί, ας, ἡ** Cenchreae, the port of Corinth on the Saronic Gulf.

## Κεδρών
**Κεδρών, ὁ** Kidron, a valley near Jerusalem.

## κεῖμαι
**κεῖμαι** I lie, recline, am placed, am laid, set, specially appointed, destined.

## κειρία
**κειρία, ας, ἡ** a kind of girdle made of cords; a bandage, grave clothes.

## κείρω
**κείρω** I shear, cut the hair of; mid: I cut my own hair, have my hair cut.

## κέλευσμα
**κέλευσμα, ατος, τό** a word of command, a call, an arousing outcry.

## κελεύω
**κελεύω** I command, order, direct, bid.

## κενοδοξία
**κενοδοξία, ας, ἡ** vainglory, empty pride.

## κενόδοξος
**κενόδοξας, ον** vainglorious, boastful.

## κενός
**κενός, ή, όν** (a) empty, (b) met: empty (in moral content), vain, ineffective, foolish, worthless, (c) false, unreal, pretentious, hollow.

## κενοφωνία
**κενοφωνία, ας, ἡ** empty disputing, worthless babble.

## κενόω
**κενόω** (a) I empty, (b) I deprive of content, make unreal.

## κέντρον
**κέντρον, ου, τό** a sting, goad; met: of death.

## κεντυρίων
**κεντυρίων, ῶνος, ὁ** a centurion, an officer commanding about a hundred infantry in the Roman army.

## κενῶς
**κενῶς** falsely, in vain, to no purpose.

## κεραία
**κεραία, ας, ἡ** a little hook, an apostrophe on letters of the alphabet, distinguishing them from other little letters, or a separation stroke between letters.

## κεραμεύς
**κεραμεύς, έως, ὁ** a potter.

## κεραμικός
**κεραμικός, ή, όν** of clay, made by a potter, earthen.

## κεράμιον
**κεράμιον, ου, τό** a pitcher, earthen vessel, jar.

## κέραμος
**κέραμος, ου, ὁ** a tile; practically: the roof.

## κεράννυμι
**κεράννυμι** I mix, mingle, pour out for drinking.

## κέρας
**κέρας, ατος, τό** (a) a horn, (b) a horn-like projection at the corner of an altar, (c) a horn as a symbol of power.

## κεράτιον
**κεράτιον, ου, τό** a husk (pod) of the carob.

## κερδαίνω
**κερδαίνω** I gain, acquire, win (over), avoid loss.

## κέρδος
**κέρδος, ους, τό** gain, advantage, profit.

## κέρμα
**κέρμα, ατος, τό** a small coin; plur: small change.

## κερματιστής
**κερματιστής, οῦ, ὁ** properly: a changer of large into smaller coins, a money-changer.

## κεφάλαιον
**κεφάλαιον, ου, τό** (a) the chief matter, the main point, (b) a sum of money.

## κεφαλή
**κεφαλή, ῆς, ἡ** (a) the head, (b) met: a corner stone, uniting two walls; head, ruler, lord.

## κεφαλιόω
**κεφαλαιόω** I wound in the head.

## κεφαλίς
**κεφαλίς, ίδος, ἡ** (lit: little head, then: the knob at the end of the wooden core of a roll of papyrus, then) a roll, volume, division (of a book).

## κημόω
**φιμόω** I muzzle, silence.

## κῆνσος
**κῆνσος, ου, ὁ** a poll-tax.

## κῆπος
**κῆπος, ου, ὁ** a garden, any place planted with trees and herbs.

## κηπουρός
**κηπουρός, οῦ, ὁ** a gardener, garden-keeper.

## κήρυγμα
**κήρυγμα, ατος, τό** a proclamation, preaching.

## κῆρυξ
**κῆρυξ, υκος, ὁ** a herald, preacher, proclaimer.

## κηρύσσω
**κηρύσσω** I proclaim, herald, preach.

## κῆτος
**κῆτος, ους, τό** a sea monster, huge sea fish, whale.

## Κηφᾶς
**Κηφᾶς, ᾶ, ὁ** Cephas (Aramaic for rock), the new name given to Simon Peter, the apostle.

## κιβωτός
**κιβωτός, οῦ, ἡ** (properly: a wooden box, hence) the Ark, in which Noah sailed; the Ark of the Covenant.

## κιθάρα
**κιθάρα, ας, ἡ** a harp, lyre.

## κιθαρίζω
**κιθαρίζω** intrans. and trans: I play on the harp, I harp, with acc. of the tune.

## κιθαρῳδός
**κιθαρῳδός, ου, ὁ** a harpist, one who sings with harp as accompaniment.

## Κιλικία
**Κιλικία, ας, ἡ** Cilicia, a Roman province between the Taurus range of mountains and the coast in the south-east corner of Asia Minor, linked up with the province of Syria.

## κινδυνεύω
**κινδυνεύω** I am in danger or peril.

## κίνδυνος
**κίνδυνος, ου, ὁ** danger, peril, risk.

## κινέω
**κινέω** I set in motion, move, remove, excite, stir up.

## κιννάμωμον
**κινάμωμον, ου, τό** (a Semitic word) cinnamon.

## Κίς
**Κίς, ὁ** Kish, the father of Saul.

## κίχρημι
**χράω** I lend.

## κλάδος
**κλάδος, ου, ὁ** a young tender shoot, then: a branch; met: of descendants.

## κλαίω
**κλαίω** I weep, weep for, mourn, lament.

## κλάσις
**κλάσις, εως, ἡ** a breaking.

## κλάσμα
**κλάσμα, ατος, τό** a fragment, broken piece.

## Κλαυδία
**Κλαυδία, ας, ἡ** Claudia, a Christian woman in Rome; probably a freedwoman of the imperial household.

## Κλαύδιος
**Κλαύδιος, ου, ὁ** (a) Claudius, the fourth of the Roman Emperors, Tiberius Claudius Caesar Augustus Germanicus, who ruled A.D. 41-54, (b) Claudius Lysias, a tribune at Jerusalem.

## κλαυθμός
**κλαυθμός, οῦ, ὁ** weeping, lamentation, crying.

## κλάω
**κλάω** I break (in pieces), break bread.

## κλείς
**κλεῖς, κλειδός, ἡ** a key.

## κλείω
**κλείω** I shut, shut up.

## κλέμμα
**κλέμμα, ατος, τό** a theft.

## Κλεοπᾶς
**Κλεοπᾶς, ᾶ, ὁ** Cleopas, one of the two companions of the risen Jesus from Jerusalem to Emmaus.

## κλέος
**κλέος, ους, τό** glory, fame, praise; rumor, report, credit.

## κλέπτης
**κλέπτης, ου, ὁ** a thief.

## κλέπτω
**κλέπτω** I steal.

## κλῆμα
**κλῆμα, ατος, τό** a branch, shoot, twig.

## Κλήμης
**Κλήμης, μεντος, ὁ** Clement, a fellow-worker of Paul in Rome.

## κληρονομέω
**κληρονομέω** I inherit, obtain (possess) by inheritance, acquire.

## κληρονομία
**κληρονομία, ας, ἡ** an inheritance, an heritage, regularly the gift of God to His chosen people, in the Old Testament: the Promised Land, in NT a possession viewed in one sense as present, in another as future; a share, participation.

## κληρονόμος
**κληρονόμος, ου, ὁ** an heir, an inheritor.

## κλῆρος
**κλῆρος, ου, ὁ** (a) a lot, (b) a portion assigned; hence: a portion of the people of God assigned to one's care, a congregation.

## κληρόω
**κληρόω** I cast lots, choose by lot, assign by lot, assign a portion, receive a share.

## κλῆσις
**κλῆσις, εως, ἡ** a calling, invitation; in the NT, always of a divine call.

## κλητός
**κλητός, ή, όν** called, invited, summoned by God to an office or to salvation.

## κλίβανος
**κλίβανος, ου, ὁ** an oven, furnace.

## κλίμα
**κλίμα, ατος, τό** a small geographical division, district, or territory.

## κλινάριον
**κλίνη, ης, ἡ** a couch, bed, portable bed or mat, a couch for reclining at meals, possibly also a bier.

## κλίνη
**κλίνη, ης, ἡ** a couch, bed, portable bed or mat, a couch for reclining at meals, possibly also a bier.

## κλινίδιον
**κλινίδιον, ου, τό** a couch or litter of a sick person.

## κλίνω
**κλίνω** trans: I rest, recline; I bend, incline; I cause to give ground, make to yield; intrans: I decline, approach my end.

## κλισία
**κλισία, ας, ἡ** properly: a dining couch; hence: a group of diners.

## κλοπή
**κλοπή, ῆς, ἡ** thieving, theft.

## κλύδων
**κλύδων, ῶνος, ὁ** rough water, a wave, billow, surge.

## κλυδωνίζομαι
**κλυδωνίζομαι** I am tossed by waves, met: I am tossed to and fro.

## Κλωπᾶς
**Κλωπᾶς, ᾶ, ὁ** Clopas, husband of one Mary, who stood by the cross.

## κνήθομαι
**κνήθω** I rub, tickle, scratch; pass: I itch.

## Κνίδος
**Κνίδος, ου, ἡ** Cnidus, a town on the coast of Caria (south-west Asia Minor) near the island of Cos.

## κοδράντης
**κοδράντης, ου, ὁ** a "quadrans," the smallest Roman copper coin, a quarter of an "as," the sixteenth part of a "sesterius".

## κοιλία
**κοιλία, ας, ἡ** belly, abdomen, heart, a general term covering any organ in the abdomen, e.g. stomach, womb; met: the inner man.

## κοιμάομαι
**κοιμάομαι** I fall asleep, am asleep, sometimes of the sleep of death.

## κοίμησις
**κοίμησις, εως, ἡ** repose, taking rest, sleep.

## κοινός
**κοινός, ή, όν** (a) common, shared, (b) Hebraistic use: profane; dirty, unclean, unwashed.

## κοινόω
**κοινόω** I make unclean, pollute, desecrate, mid: I regard (treat) as unclean.

## κοινωνέω
**κοινωνέω** (a) I share, communicate, contribute, impart, (b) I share in, have a share of, have fellowship with.

## κοινωνία
**κοινωνία, ας, ἡ** (lit: partnership) (a) contributory help, participation, (b) sharing in, communion, (c) spiritual fellowship, a fellowship in the spirit.

## κοινωνικός
**κοινωνικός, ή, όν** willing to share, sociable, ready to communicate, beneficent.

## κοινωνός
**κοινωνός, οῦ, ὁ, ἡ** a sharer, partner, companion.

## κοίτη
**κοίτη, ης, ἡ** (a) a bed, (b) a marriage bed; plur: repeated (immoral) sexual intercourse.

## κοιτών
**κοιτών, ῶνος, ὁ** a bed-chamber.

## κόκκινος
**κόκκινος, η, ον** crimson, scarlet, dyed with Kermes (coccum), the female coccus of the Kermes oak.

## κόκκος
**κόκκος, ου, ὁ** a kernel, grain, seed.

## κολάζω
**κολάζω** I chastise, curtail, punish; mid: I cause to be punished.

## κολακεία
**κολακεία, ας, ἡ** flattery, with a view to advantage or gain.

## κόλασις
**κόλασις, εως, ἡ** chastisement, punishment, torment, perhaps with the idea of deprivation.

## κολαφίζω
**κολαφίζω** I strike with the fist, buffet; hence: I mistreat violently.

## κολλάομαι
**κολλάω** (lit: I glue); hence: mid. and pass: I join myself closely, cleave, adhere (to), I keep company (with), of friendly intercourse.

## κολλούριον
**κολλούριον, ου, τό** eye-salve.

## κολλυβιστής
**κολλυβιστής, ου, ὁ** a money-changer, who changed heathen into Jewish money, for payment into the Temple treasury.

## κολοβόω
**κολοβόω** (lit: I maim, mutilate), I cut short, shorten, abbreviate.

## Κολοσσαί
**Κολοσσαί, ῆς, ἡ** Colossae, a town of the Roman province Asia, in the Lycus valley, near Laodicea and Hierapolis.

## κόλπος
**κόλπος, ου, ὁ** (a) sing. and plur: bosom; (sinus) the overhanging fold of the garment used as a pocket, (b) a bay, gulf.

## κολυμβάω
**κολυμβάω** (properly: I dive, hence) I swim.

## κολυμβήθρα
**κολυμβήθρα, ας, ἡ** (lit: a diving or swimming place), a pool.

## κολωνία
**κολωνία, ας, ἡ** a colony, a city settlement of Roman (soldier) citizens; a garrison city.

## κομάω
**κομάω** I wear the hair long, allow the hair to grow out.

## κόμη
**κόμη, ης, ἡ** hair, long hair.

## κομίζω
**κομίζω** (a) act: I convey, bring, carry, (b) mid: I receive back, receive what has belonged to myself but has been lost, or else promised but kept back, or: I get what has come to be my own by earning, recover.

## κομψότερον
**κομψότερον** in better health.

## κονιάω
**κονιάω** I whitewash, plaster over.

## κονιορτός
**κονιορτός, ου, ὁ** dust.

## κοπάζω
**κοπάζω** I abate, cease raging, am stilled.

## κοπετός
**κοπετός, οῦ, ὁ** beating of the breast or head in lamentation, lamentation.

## κοπή
**κοπή, ῆς, ἡ** slaughter, smiting in battle.

## κοπιάω
**κοπιάω** (a) I grow weary, (b) I toil, work with effort (of bodily and mental labor alike).

## κόπος
**κόπος, ου, ὁ** (a) trouble, (b) toil, labor, laborious toil, involving weariness and fatigue.

## κοπρία
**κοπρία, ας, ἡ** manure; a dung-hill.

## κόπριον
**κοπρία, ας, ἡ** manure; a dung-hill.

## κόπτω
**κόπτω** (a) I cut, cut off, strike, smite, (b) mid: I beat my breast or head in lamentation, lament, mourn, sometimes with acc. of person whose loss is mourned.

## κόραξ
**κόραξ, ός, ὁ** a raven, crow.

## κοράσιον
**κοράσιον, ου, τό** a little girl, a young girl; a girl, maiden.

## Κορβᾶν
**κορβᾶν** a gift, offering, anything consecrated to God.

## κορβανᾶς
**κορβᾶν** a gift, offering, anything consecrated to God.

## Κόρε
**Κορέ, ὁ** (Hebrew) Korah.

## κορέννυμι
**κορέννυμι** I fill, sate, glut, feed full, satisfy.

## Κορίνθιος
**Κορίνθιος, ου, ὁ** Corinthian, of Corinth.

## Κόρινθος
**Κόρινθος, ου, ἡ** Corinth, in north-east Peloponnese, the capital of the Roman province Achaia.

## Κορνήλιος
**Κορνήλιος, ου, ὁ** Cornelius, a centurion of the Roman army, stationed at Caesarea.

## κόρος
**κόρος, ου, ὁ** (Hebrew) a (dry) measure, equivalent to 120 gallons.

## κοσμέω
**κοσμέω** I put into order; I decorate, deck, adorn.

## κοσμικός
**κοσμικός, ή, όν** earthly, worldly (belonging to the present earthly world as opposed to the heavenly and future).

## κόσμιος
**κόσμιος, ία, ον** orderly, virtuous, decent, modest, well-ordered.

## κοσμοκράτωρ
**κοσμοκράτωρ, ορος, ὁ** ruler of this world, that is, of the world as asserting its independence of God; used of the angelic or demonic powers controlling the sublunary world.

## κόσμος
**κόσμος, ου, ὁ** the world, universe; worldly affairs; the inhabitants of the world; adornment.

## Κούαρτος
**Κούαρτος, ου, ὁ** Quartus, a Christian, brother of Erastus the Corinthian.

## κουμ
**κοῦμι** (Aramaic) arise, stand up.

## κουστωδία
**κουστωδία, ας, ἡ** a guard, watch.

## κουφίζω
**κουφίζω** I lighten, make light.

## κόφινος
**κόφινος, ου, ὁ** a large basket.

## κράβαττος
**κράβαττος, ου, ὁ** a bed, mattress, mat of a poor man.

## κράζω
**κράζω** I cry aloud, shriek.

## κραιπάλη
**κραιπάλη, ης, ἡ** drunken dissipation, surfeiting.

## κρανίον
**κρανίον, ου, τό** a skull.

## κράσπεδον
**κράσπεδον, ου, τό** the fringe, edge, corner, tassel.

## κραταιόομαι
**κραταιόω** I strengthen, confirm; pass: I grow strong, become strong.

## κραταιός
**κραταιός, ά, όν** strong, powerful, mighty.

## κρατέω
**κρατέω** I am strong, mighty, hence: I rule, am master, prevail; I obtain, take hold of; I hold, hold fast.

## κράτιστος
**κράτιστος, η, ον** most excellent, an official epithet, used in addressing a Roman of high rank, and in the second century one of equestrian (as distinguished from senatorial) rank.

## κράτος
**κράτος, ους, τό** dominion, strength, power; a mighty deed.

## κραυγάζω
**κραυγάζω** I cry aloud, shout, exclaim.

## κραυγή
**κραυγή, ῆς, ἡ** (a) a shout, cry, clamor, (b) outcry, clamoring against another.

## κρέας
**κρέας, έως, τό** flesh; plur: pieces of flesh, kinds of flesh.

## κρείττων
**κρείττων, ον** stronger, more excellent.

## κρεμάννυμι
**κρεμάννυμι** I hang, hang up, suspend; mid: I am hanging, hang.

## κρημνός
**κρημνός, οῦ, ὁ** a crag, precipice, steep bank.

## Κρής
**Κρής, ητός, ὁ** a Cretan, an inhabitant of Crete.

## Κρήσκης
**Κρήσκης, κεντος, ὁ** Crescens, a Christian, coadjutor of Paul.

## Κρήτη
**Κρήτη, ης, ἡ** Crete.

## κριθή
**κριθή, ῆς, ἡ** barley.

## κρίθινος
**κρίθινος, η, ον** made of barley.

## κρίμα
**κρίμα, ατος, τό** (a) a judgment, a verdict; sometimes implying an adverse verdict, a condemnation, (b) a case at law, a lawsuit.

## κρίνον
**κρίνον, ου, τό** a lily growing wild, variously identified with the red anemone, the whole lily, the sword lily.

## κρίνω
**κρίνω** (a) I judge, whether in a law-court or privately: sometimes with cognate nouns emphasizing the notion of the verb, (b) I decide, I think (it) good.

## κρίσις
**κρίσις, εως, ἡ** judging, judgment, decision, sentence; generally: divine judgment; accusation.

## Κρίσπος
**Κρίσπος, ου, ὁ** Crispus, ruler of the synagogue at Corinth, converted and baptized by Paul.

## κριτήριον
**κριτήριον, ου, τό** criterion; a law-court; a law-case before an arbiter; a cause, controversy.

## κριτής
**κριτής, ου, ὁ** a judge, magistrate, ruler.

## κριτικός
**κριτικός, ή, όν** critical, able to judge or discern.

## κρούω
**κρούω** I knock, beat a door with a stick, to gain admittance.

## κρύπτη
**κρύπτη, ης, ἡ** a cellar, vault, hidden place, crypt.

## κρυπτός
**κρυπτός, ή, όν** hidden, secret; as subst: the hidden (secret) things (parts), the inward nature (character).

## κρύπτω
**κρύπτω** I hide, conceal, lay up.

## κρυσταλλίζω
**κρυσταλλίζω** I am clear as crystal, brilliant like crystal.

## κρύσταλλος
**κρύσταλλος, ου, ὁ** crystal.

## κρυφαῖος
**κρυπτός, ή, όν** hidden, secret; as subst: the hidden (secret) things (parts), the inward nature (character).

## κρυφῇ
**κρυφῇ** in secret, secretly.

## κτάομαι
**κτάομαι** (a) I acquire, win, get, purchase, buy, (b) I possess, win mastery over.

## κτῆμα
**κτῆμα, ατος, τό** a piece of landed property, a field; plur: possessions, property, possibly landed property, property in land.

## κτῆνος
**κτῆνος, ους, τό** a beast of burden (generally, a horse or mule) either for riding or for carrying loads on its back, or for yoking to a cart or carriage.

## κτήτωρ
**κτήτωρ, ορος, ὁ** a possessor, owner.

## κτίζω
**κτίζω** I create, form, shape, make, always of God.

## κτίσις
**κτίσις, εως, ἡ** (often of the founding of a city), (a) abstr: creation, (b) concr: creation, creature, institution; always of Divine work, (c) an institution, ordinance.

## κτίσμα
**κτίσμα, ατος, τό** a created thing, a creature.

## κτίστης
**κτίστης, ου, ὁ** (often of the founder of a city), creator, God.

## κυβεία
**κυβεία, ας, ἡ** (lit: playing with dice, gaming, hence) trickery, sleight.

## κυβέρνησις
**κυβέρνησις, εως, ἡ** (lit: steering, piloting), governing, government.

## κυβερνήτης
**κυβερνήτης, ου, ὁ** a steersman, pilot; met: a guide, governor.

## κυκλεύω
**κυκλόω** I encircle, besiege, surround.

## κυκλόθεν
**κυκλόθεν** in a circle round, round about.

## κυκλόω
**κυκλόω** I encircle, besiege, surround.

## κύκλῳ
**κύκλος, ου, ὁ** a circle, ring.

## κυλίομαι
**κυλίω** I roll, roll along, wallow.

## κυλισμός
**κύλισμα, ατος, τό** a place of wallowing.

## κυλλός
**κυλλός, ή, όν** crippled, lame, especially in the hands.

## κῦμα
**κῦμα, ατος, τό** a wave, surge, billow.

## κύμβαλον
**κύμβαλον, ου, τό** a cymbal.

## κύμινον
**κύμινον, ου, τό** cumin, a plant used as a spice.

## κυνάριον
**κυνάριον, ου, τό** a little dog, a house dog.

## Κύπριος
**Κύπριος, ου, ὁ** a Cypriote, belonging to Cyprus.

## Κύπρος
**Κύπρος, ου, ἡ** Cyprus.

## κύπτω
**κύπτω** I bend, stoop down, bow the head.

## Κυρηναῖος
**Κυρηναῖος, ου, ὁ** belonging to Cyrene, a Cyrenaean.

## Κυρήνη
**Κυρήνη, ης, ἡ** Cyrene, a district west of Egypt on the Mediterranean coast, forming with Crete a Roman province.

## Κυρήνιος
**Κυρήνιος, ου, ὁ** Cyrenius or Quirinius, governor of Syria.

## κυρία
**κυρία, ας, ἡ** a lady.

## κυριακός
**κυριακός, ή, όν** of the Lord, special to the Lord.

## κυριεύω
**κυριεύω** I have authority, rule over.

## κύριος
**κύριος, ου, ὁ** lord, master, sir; the Lord.

## κυριότης
**κυριότης, τητος, ἡ** (a) abstr: lordship, (b) concr: divine or angelic lordship, domination, dignity, usually with reference to a celestial hierarchy.

## κυρόω
**κυρόω** I ratify, confirm, make valid, reaffirm, assure.

## κύων
**κύων, κυνός, ὁ, ἡ** a dog, universally despised in the East.

## κῶλον
**κῶλον, ου, τό** a limb, member of a body; fig: a corpse, carcass.

## κωλύω
**κωλύω** I prevent, debar, hinder; with infin: from doing so and so.

## κώμη
**κώμη, ης, ἡ** a village, country town.

## κωμόπολις
**κωμόπολις, εως, ἡ** a large village, a city which in constitution has only the status of a village; a country town.

## κῶμος
**κῶμος, ου, ὁ** a feasting, reveling, carousal.

## κώνωψ
**κώνωψ, ωπος, ὁ** a gnat, mosquito, referred to proverbially as something small.

## Κώς
**Κῶς, ῶ, ἡ** Cos, an island in the Aegean Sea, south-west of Asia Minor.

## Κωσάμ
**Κωσάμ, ὁ** Cosam, son of Elmadam and father of Addi.

## κωφός
**κωφός, ή, όν** (lit: blunted) dumb, dull, deaf.

## λαγχάνω
**λαγχάνω** (a) I obtain (receive) by lot, my lot (turn) is, (b) I draw lots.

## Λάζαρος
**Λάζαρος, ου, ὁ** Lazarus, Eliezer, (a) the beggar, (b) the brother of Martha and Mary, of Bethany.

## λάθρᾳ
**λάθρᾳ** secretly, privately.

## λαῖλαψ
**λαῖλαψ, απος, ἡ** a sudden storm, squall, whirlwind, hurricane.

## λακάω
**λάσκω** I burst asunder with a loud noise.

## λακτίζω
**λακτίζω** I kick.

## λαλέω
**λαλέω** (I talk, chatter in classical Greek, but in NT a more dignified word) I speak, say.

## λαλιά
**λαλιά, ας, ἡ** (in classical Greek: babble, chattering) speech, talk; manner of speech, dialect.

## λαμβάνω
**λαμβάνω** (a) I receive, get, (b) I take, lay hold of.

## Λάμεχ
**Λάμεχ, ὁ** (Hebrew), Lamech, son of Methuselah and father of Noah.

## λαμπάς
**λαμπάς, άδος, ἡ** a torch, lamp, lantern.

## λαμπρός
**λαμπρός, ά, όν** shining, magnificent, bright, splendid.

## λαμπρότης
**λαμπρότης, τητος, ἡ** splendor, brightness, brilliancy.

## λαμπρῶς
**λαμπρῶς** magnificently, sumptuously, splendidly.

## λάμπω
**λάμπω** I shine, give light.

## λανθάνω
**λανθάνω** I am hidden (concealed), lie hid, escape notice, sometimes with acc. of person from whom concealment takes place; I do so and so unconsciously, unknown to myself, I shut my eyes to so and so.

## λαξευτός
**λαξευτός, ή, όν** hewn out of the rock.

## Λαοδίκεια
**Λαοδικεία, ας, ἡ** Laodicea, a city in the Lycos valley in the Roman province Asia, near Colossae and Hierapolis.

## Λαοδικεύς
**Λαοδικεύς, έως, ὁ** a Laodicean, an inhabitant of Laodicea.

## λαός
**λαός, οῦ, ὁ** (a) a people, characteristically of God's chosen people, first the Jews, then the Christians, (b) sometimes, but rarely, the people, the crowd.

## λάρυγξ
**λάρυγξ, υγγος, ὁ** the throat, gullet.

## Λασαία
**Λασαία, ας, ἡ** Lasea, a city in Crete, about the middle of the south coast.

## λατομέω
**λατομέω** I hew stones, cut stones.

## λατρεία
**λατρεία, ας, ἡ** service rendered to God, perhaps simply: worship.

## λατρεύω
**λατρεύω** I serve, especially God, perhaps simply: I worship.

## λάχανον
**λάχανον, ου, τό** an herb, garden plant, vegetable.

## λεγιών
**λεγεών, ῶνος, ἡ** properly: a division of the Roman army, numbering about 6,000 infantry with additional cavalry; hence: a very large number; a legion.

## λέγω
**λέγω** (denoting speech in progress), (a) I say, speak; I mean, mention, tell, (b) I call, name, especially in the pass., (c) I tell, command.

## λεῖμμα
**λεῖμμα, ατος, τό** a remnant, remainder.

## λεῖος
**λεῖος, α, ον** smooth, level, plain.

## λείπω
**λείπω** (earlier: I leave behind, abandon), (a) I am wanting, (b) mid: e.g. with gen: I come behind (in a race), am left behind in, fall short of (some standard), am wanting in.

## λειτουργέω
**λειτουργέω** I act in the public service, render service, minister, in the widest sense, of some special public religious service, but also of the service of priests and Levites.

## λειτουργία
**λειτουργία, ας, ἡ** a charitable gift, public service in the widest sense; service as of priest or Levite ritual.

## λειτουργικός
**λειτουργικός, ή, όν** given to serving (ministration), ministering.

## λειτουργός
**λειτουργός, ου, ὁ** a minister, servant, of an official character; of priests and Levites.

## λεμά
**λιμά** (Hebrew), why.

## λέντιον
**λέντιον, ου, τό** a towel, apron, coarse cloth.

## λεπίς
**λεπίς, ίδος, ἡ** a scale, a scaly substance thrown off from the body.

## λέπρα
**λέπρα, ας, ἡ** leprosy.

## λεπρός
**λεπρός, οῦ, ὁ** a leprous person, a leper.

## λεπτόν
**λεπτόν, οῦ, τό** a small piece of money, probably the smallest piece of money.

## Λευί
**Λευΐ, ὁ** (Hebrew), Levi, (a) an ancestor of Jesus, (b) another ancestor of Jesus, (c) third son of Jacob, the patriarch, and founder of a tribe named after him, (d) son of Alphaeus, and called Matthew, a revenue officer and one of the twelve apostles of Jesus.

## Λευίς
**Λευΐς, ὁ** (Hebrew), Levi, son of Alphaeus, the publican.

## Λευίτης
**Λευΐτης, ου, ὁ** a Levite, properly a man of the tribe of Levi; hence: a priest's assistant, an under priest, as the members of that tribe were charged with this duty.

## Λευιτικός
**Λευϊτικός, ή, όν** belonging to the tribe of Levi; Levitical.

## λευκαίνω
**λευκαίνω** I whiten, make white.

## λευκός
**λευκός, ή, όν** white, bright, brilliant.

## λέων
**λέων, οντος, ὁ** a lion.

## λήθη
**λήθη, ῆς, ἡ** forgetfulness, oblivion.

## λῆμψις
**λῆψις, εως, ἡ** a receiving.

## ληνός
**ληνός, οῦ, ὁ, ἡ** a trough, vat, winepress.

## λῆρος
**λῆρος, ου, ὁ** folly, nonsense, idle talk.

## λῃστής
**λῃστής, οῦ, ὁ** a robber, brigand, bandit.

## λίαν
**λίαν** very; very much, exceedingly, greatly.

## λίβανος
**λίβανος, ου, ὁ** (Semitic word), frankincense, incense.

## λιβανωτός
**λιβανωτός, οῦ, ὁ** a censer.

## Λιβερτῖνος
**Λιβερτῖνος, ου, ὁ** a freedman, one of the class of manumitted slaves; a synagogue at Jerusalem appears to have been reserved for them.

## Λιβύη
**Λιβύη, ης, ἡ** Libya, Africa (in the modern sense).

## λιθάζω
**λιθάζω** I stone, pelt with stones.

## λίθινος
**λίθινος, η, ον** made of stone.

## λιθοβολέω
**λιθοβολέω** I stone, cast stones (at), kill by stoning.

## λίθος
**λίθος, ου, ὁ** a stone; met: of Jesus as the chief stone in a building.

## λιθόστρωτος
**λιθόστρωτον, ου, τό** (adj: paved with stone), a mosaic pavement.

## λικμάω
**λικμάω** I crush to powder, scatter like chaff.

## Λιμήν
**λιμήν, ένος, ὁ** a harbor, port, haven.

## λιμήν
**λιμήν, ένος, ὁ** a harbor, port, haven.

## λίμνη
**λίμνη, ης, ἡ** a lake.

## λιμός
**λιμός, οῦ, ὁ, ἡ** a famine, hunger.

## λίνον
**λίνον, ου, τό** flax, linen.

## Λίνος
**Λῖνος, ου, ὁ** Linus, a Christian in Rome.

## λιπαρός
**λιπαρός, ά, όν** (lit: fat), rich, sumptuous.

## λίτρα
**λίτρα, ας, ἡ** a Roman pound, of about twelve ounces.

## λίψ
**λίψ, λιβός, ὁ** the south-west wind, and thus the quarter from which it comes.

## λογεία
**λογία, ας, ἡ** a collection, collecting (of money), particularly of an irregular local contribution for religious purposes.

## λογίζομαι
**λογίζομαι** I reckon, count, charge with; reason, decide, conclude; think, suppose.

## λογικός
**λογικός, ή, όν** (a) reasonable, rational, (b) metaphorical, as contrasted with the literal.

## λόγιον
**λόγιον, ου, τό** plur: oracles, divine responses or utterances (it can include the entire Old Testament).

## λόγιος
**λόγιος, α, ον** eloquent, gifted with learning.

## λογισμός
**λογισμός, οῦ, ὁ** reasoning, thinking; a conception, device.

## λογομαχέω
**λογομαχέω** I contend about words.

## λογομαχία
**λογομαχία, ας, ἡ** contention about words, an unprofitable controversy.

## λόγος
**λόγος, ου, ὁ** a word, speech, divine utterance, analogy.

## λόγχη
**λόγχη, ης, ἡ** a lance, spear.

## λοιδορέω
**λοιδορέω** I revile a person to his face, abuse insultingly.

## λοιδορία
**λοιδορία, ας, ἡ** reviling, abuse.

## λοίδορος
**λοίδορος, ου, ὁ** a railer, reviler, abuser.

## λοιμός
**λοιμός, οῦ, ὁ** (a) a pestilence, (b) a pestilent fellow.

## λοιπός
**λοιπός, ή, όν** left, left behind, the remainder, the rest, the others.

## Λουκᾶς
**Λουκᾶς, ᾶ, ὁ** Lucas, Luke, Christian physician and writer of the Third Gospel and Acts.

## Λούκιος
**Λούκιος, ου, ὁ** Lucius, (a) of Cyrene, an early Christian, in the church of Antioch, by some identified with the evangelist Luke, (b) a Christian with Paul at Corinth, by some identified with (a).

## λουτρόν
**λουτρόν, οῦ, τό** a bath (of water, not the vessel), water for washing, washing.

## λούω
**λούω** (lit. or merely ceremonially), I wash, bathe (the body); mid: of washing, bathing one's self; met: I cleanse from sin.

## Λύδδα
**Λύδδα, Λύδδης, ἡ** Lydda, Diospolis, Lod (modern Ludd), a city on the way to Joppa within a day's journey of Jerusalem.

## Λυδία
**Λυδία, ας, ἡ** Lydia, a lady resident of Philippi, native of Thyatira in Lydia (Asia Minor), and engaged in the clothing trade.

## Λυκαονία
**Λυκαονία, ας, ἡ** Lycaonia, the country of the Lykaones, a district of Asia Minor, comprised within the Roman province Galatia and including the cities of Derbe and Lystra.

## Λυκαονιστί
**Λυκαονιστί** in the Lycaonian language.

## Λυκία
**Λυκία, ας, ἡ** Lycia, a small Roman province on the south coast of Asia Minor.

## λύκος
**λύκος, ου, ὁ** a wolf, of perhaps a jackal; often applied to persons of wolfish proclivities.

## λυμαίνομαι
**λυμαίνομαι** I outrage, maltreat, corrupt, defile.

## λυπέω
**λυπέω** I pain, grieve, vex.

## λύπη
**λύπη, ης, ἡ** pain, grief, sorrow, affliction.

## Λυσανίας
**Λυσανίας, ου, ὁ** Lysanias, tetrarch of Abilene.

## Λυσίας
**Λυσίας, ου, ὁ** Claudius Lysias, a Roman tribune of the soldiers in Jerusalem.

## λύσις
**λύσις, εως, ἡ** dissolution, release; a loosing, divorce.

## λυσιτελεῖ
**λυσιτελέω** it is advantageous to, it profits.

## Λύστρα
**Λύστρα, ας, ἡ, ων, τά** Lystra, a Lycaonian city in the southern part of the Roman province Galatia.

## λύτρον
**λύτρον, ου, τό** the purchasing money for manumitting slaves, a ransom, the price of ransoming; especially the sacrifice by which expiation is effected, an offering of expiation.

## λυτρόομαι
**λυτρόω** I release on receipt of ransom; mid: I redeem, release by paying ransom, liberate.

## λύτρωσις
**λύτρωσις, εως, ἡ** (in the Old Testament: ransoming from imprisonment for debt, or from slavery, release from national misfortune, etc.), liberation, deliverance, release.

## λυτρωτής
**λυτρωτής, οῦ, ὁ** a redeemer, liberator, deliverer.

## λυχνία
**λυχνία, ας, ἡ** a lamp-stand.

## λύχνος
**λύχνος, ου, ὁ** a lamp.

## λύω
**λύω** (a) I loose, untie, release, (b) met: I break, destroy, set at naught, contravene; I break up a meeting, annul.

## Λωΐς
**Λωΐς, ΐδος, ἡ** Lois, grandmother of Timothy.

## Λώτ
**Λώτ, ὁ** (Hebrew), Lot, nephew of Abraham.

## Μάαθ
**Μαάθ, ὁ** (Hebrew), Maath, an ancestor of Jesus.

## Μαγαδάν
**Μαγδαλά, ἡ** Magdala, Magadan, a proper name.

## Μαγδαληνή
**Μαγδαληνή, ῆς, ἡ** Magdalene, a woman of Magdala.

## μαγεία
**μαγεία, ας, ἡ** magic.

## μαγεύω
**μαγεύω** I practice sorcery or magic.

## μάγος
**μάγος, ου, ὁ** a sorcerer, a magician, a wizard.

## Μαγώγ
**Μαγώγ, ὁ** (Hebrew), Magog, sometimes as name of a people, sometimes as name of a country in the Old Testament, probably the Scythians; hence: used in apocalyptic literature.

## Μαδιάμ
**Μαδιάμ, ἡ** (Hebrew), Midian, generally taken to mean or to include the peninsula of Sinai.

## μαθητεύω
**μαθητεύω** I make a disciple of, train in discipleship; pass: I am trained, discipled, instructed.

## μαθητής
**μαθητής, οῦ, ὁ** a learner, disciple, pupil.

## μαθήτρια
**μαθήτρια, ας, ἡ** a female disciple, female Christian.

## Μαθθαῖος
**Ματθαῖος, ου, ὁ** Matthew.

## Μαθθάτ
**Ματθάτ, ὁ** Matthat, an ancestor of Jesus.

## Μαθθίας
**Ματθίας, α, ὁ** Matthias.

## Μαθουσαλά
**Μαθουσάλα, ὁ** (Hebrew) Methuselah, son of Enoch and father of Lamech.

## μαίνομαι
**μαίνομαι** I am raving mad, speak as a madman.

## μακαρίζω
**μακαρίζω** I bless, pronounce blessed or happy.

## μακάριος
**μακάριος, α, ον** happy, blessed, to be envied.

## μακαρισμός
**μακαρισμός, οῦ, ὁ** regarding as happy, blessed, or enviable.

## Μακεδονία
**Μακεδονία, ας, ἡ** (Hebrew), Macedonia, a Roman province north of Achaia (Greece).

## Μακεδών
**Μακεδών, όνος, ὁ** a Macedonian, an inhabitant of the Roman province Macedonia.

## μάκελλον
**μάκελλον, ου, τό** a meat-market, marketplace.

## μακράν
**μακράν** at a distance, far away, remote, alien.

## μακρόθεν
**μακρόθεν** from a (long) distance, afar.

## μακροθυμέω
**μακροθυμέω** I suffer long, have patience, am forbearing, perseverance.

## μακροθυμία
**μακροθυμία, ας, ἡ** patience, forbearance, longsuffering.

## μακροθύμως
**μακροθύμως** with longsuffering, patiently.

## μακρός
**μακρός, ά, όν** long, distant, far; of long duration.

## μακροχρόνιος
**μακροχρόνιος, ον** long-timed, long-lived.

## μαλακία
**μαλακία, ας, ἡ** weakness, illness, sickness.

## μαλακός
**μαλακός, ή, όν** (a) soft, (b) of persons: soft, delicate, effeminate.

## Μαλελεήλ
**Μαλελεήλ, ὁ** Mahalaleel or Maleleel, one of the ancestors of Jesus.

## μάλιστα
**μάλιστα** most of all, especially.

## μᾶλλον
**μᾶλλον** more, rather.

## Μάλχος
**Μάλχος, ου, ὁ** Malchus, a servant of the high-priest at Jerusalem.

## μάμμη
**μάμμη, ης, ἡ** a grandmother.

## μαμωνᾶς
**μαμωνᾶς, ᾶ, ὁ** (Aramaic), riches, money, possessions, property.

## Μαναήν
**Μαναήν, ὁ** (Graecized form of Aramaic Menahem), Manaen, probably a member of Herod Antipas' court.

## Μανασσῆς
**Μανασσῆς, ῆ, ὁ** (Hebrew), Manasseh, (a) son of Joseph, founder of a tribe of Israel, (b) son of Hezekiah and father of Amon (Amos).

## μανθάνω
**μανθάνω** I learn; with adj. or nouns: I learn to be so and so; with acc. of person who is the object of knowledge; aor. sometimes: to ascertain.

## μανία
**μανία, ας, ἡ** raving madness, frenzy, insanity.

## μάννα
**μάννα, τό** (Hebrew), manna, the supernatural food eaten by the Israelites in the desert: of spiritual food.

## μαντεύομαι
**μαντεύομαι** I divine, practice soothsaying, suggesting the fraud involved in the practice.

## μαραίνομαι
**μαραίνω** pass: I die, wither (like the grass).

## μαράνα
**μαρὰν ἀθά** (Aramaic), either: Our Lord hath come, or: Our Lord cometh (will come, is at hand).

## μαργαρίτης
**μαργαρίτης, ου, ὁ** a pearl.

## Μάρθα
**Μάρθα, ας, ἡ** Martha, sister of Mary and Lazarus of Bethany.

## Μαρία
**Μαρία, ας, ἡ** Mary, Miriam, (a) the mother of Jesus, (b) of Magdala, (c) sister of Martha and Lazarus, (d) wife of Cleopas, (e) mother of John Mark, (f) a Christian woman in Rome.

## Μαριάμ
**Μαρία, ας, ἡ** Mary, Miriam, (a) the mother of Jesus, (b) of Magdala, (c) sister of Martha and Lazarus, (d) wife of Cleopas, (e) mother of John Mark, (f) a Christian woman in Rome.

## Μᾶρκος
**Μάρκος, ου, ὁ** Mark, who also had the Hebrew name John, son of Mary, nephew of Barnabas, coadjutor of Barnabas, Paul, and Peter.

## μάρμαρος
**μάρμαρος, ου, ὁ, ἡ** marble.

## μαρτυρέω
**μαρτυρέω** I witness, bear witness, give evidence, testify, give a good report.

## μαρτυρία
**μαρτυρία, ας, ἡ** witness, evidence, testimony, reputation.

## μαρτύριον
**μαρτύριον, ου, τό** witness, evidence, testimony, proof.

## μαρτύρομαι
**μαρτύρομαι** (properly: I call (summon) to witness, and then absolutely) I testify, protest, asseverate; I conjure, solemnly charge.

## μάρτυς
**μάρτυς, υρος, ὁ** a witness; an eye- or ear-witness.

## μασάομαι
**μασσάομαι** I bite, gnaw, chew.

## μαστιγόω
**μαστιγόω** I flog, scourge, the victim being strapped to a pole or frame; met: I chastise.

## μαστίζω
**μαστίζω** I flog, scourge, whip.

**μάστιξ, ιγος, ἡ** (a) a scourge, lash, of leathern thongs with pieces of metal sewn up in them, (b) met: severe pains (sufferings), disease.

## μαστός
**μαστός, οῦ, ὁ** the breast, pap.

**ματαιολογία, ας, ἡ** vain speaking, foolish talking.

## ματαιολόγος

## ματαιόομαι

## μάταιος

## ματαιότης
**ματαιότης, τητος, ἡ** vanity, emptiness, unreality, purposelessness, ineffectiveness, instability, frailty; false religion.

## μάτην

**Ματθάν, ὁ** Matthan.

## Ματταθά
**Ματταθά, ὁ** Mattatha.

## Ματταθίας
**Ματταθίας, ου, ὁ** Mattathias, an ancestor of Jesus.

## μάχαιρα
**μάχαιρα, ας, ἡ** a sword.

## μάχη
**μάχη, ης, ἡ** (earlier: a battle, conflict, hence) in the sphere of words, etc: strife, contention, quarrel.

## μάχομαι
**μάχομαι** I engage in battle, fight; hence: I strive, contend, dispute.

## μεγαλεῖος
**μεγαλεῖος, εία, εῖον** grand, magnificent, splendid.

## μεγαλειότης
**μεγαλειότης, τητος, ἡ** (divine) majesty or magnificence, glory.

## μεγαλοπρεπής
**μεγαλοπρεπής, ές** magnificent, superb, transcendent, majestic.

## μεγαλύνω

## μεγάλως

## μεγαλωσύνη
**μεγαλωσύνη, ης, ἡ** (divine) majesty, greatness.

## μέγας

## μέγεθος

## μεγιστάν
**μεγιστάν, ᾶνος, ὁ** a great one, a lord; a courtier, satrap, nobleman.

## μέγιστος

**μεθερμηνεύω** I translate (from one language into another), interpret.

## μέθη
**μέθη, ης, ἡ** deep drinking, drunkenness.

## μεθίστημι
**μεθίστημι** I cause to change its place, move out of its place; I translate, transfer, remove.

## μεθοδεία
**μεθοδεία, ας, ἡ** (a way of search after something, an inquiry; a method), scheming, craftiness, deceit.

## μέθυσος
**μέθυσος, ου, ὁ** a drunkard.

## μεθύω

## μειζότερος
**μειζότερος, α, ον** greater.

## μείζων
**μείζων** greater, greatest.

## μέλαν
**μέλαν, ανος, τό** ink.

## μέλας
**μέλας, αινα, αν** black.

## Μελεά
**Μελεᾶς, ᾶ, ὁ** Melea, one of the ancestors of Jesus.

## μέλει

## μελετάω
**μελετάω** I devise, plan; practice, exercise myself in, study, ponder.

## μέλι
**μέλι, ιτος, τό** honey.

## Μελίτη
**Μελίτη, ης, ἡ** Melita, now Malta.

## μέλλω

## μέλος
**μέλος, ους, τό** a bodily organ, limb, member.

## Μελχί
**Μελχί, ὁ** Melchi, one of the ancestors of Jesus.

## Μελχισέδεκ
**Μελχισεδέκ, ὁ** Melchizedek, king and priest of Salem.

## μεμβράνα
**μεμβράνα, ης, ἡ** a parchment leaf, perhaps for notes.

## μέμφομαι
**μέμφομαι** I blame, censure, find fault.

## μεμψίμοιρος
**μεμψίμοιρος, ον** blaming one's lot or destiny, discontented, complaining.

## μέν
**μέν** an untranslatable particle, generally answered by "de", each of the two introducing a clause intended to be contrasted with the other.

## Μεννά
**Μαϊνάν, ὁ** Menna, Mainan, a proper name.

## μενοῦν
**μενοῦνγε** nay rather; indeed, truly, really.

## μενοῦνγε
**μενοῦνγε** nay rather; indeed, truly, really.

## μέντοι
**μέντοι** (a) indeed, really, (b) yet, however, nevertheless.

## μένω

## μερίζω

## μέριμνα
**μέριμνα, ης, ἡ** care, worry, anxiety.

## μεριμνάω
**μεριμνάω** I am over-anxious; with acc: I am anxious about, distracted; I care for.

## μερίς
**μερίς, ίδος, ἡ** (a) a part, division of a country, (b) a share, portion.

## μερισμός
**μερισμός, οῦ, ὁ** (a) a distributing, a distribution, (b) a parting, dividing, severance, separation.

## μεριστής
**μεριστής, οῦ, ὁ** a divider, partitioner, distributor.

## μέρος
**μέρος, ους, τό** a part, portion, share.

## μεσημβρία
**μεσημβρία, ας, ἡ** (lit: midday, hence, the position of the sun at midday), the South.

## μεσιτεύω

## μεσίτης
**μεσίτης, ου, ὁ** (a) a mediator, intermediary, (b) a go-between, arbiter, agent of something good.

## μεσονύκτιον
**μεσονύκτιον, ου, τό** midnight, the middle of the period between sunset and sunrise.

## Μεσοποταμία
**Μεσοποταμία, ας, ἡ** Mesopotamia, the Country between the (two) Rivers, i.e. the Euphrates and the Tigris.

## μέσος
**μέσος, η, ον** middle, in the middle, between, in the midst of.

## μεσότοιχον
**μεσότοιχον, ου, τό** a middle wall, partition wall, barrier.

## μεσουράνημα
**μεσουράνημα, ατος, τό** mid-heaven, the middle of heaven, the zenith.

## μεσόω
**μεσόω** I am in the middle, am advanced midway.

## Μεσσίας
**Μεσσίας, ου, ὁ** Messiah, the Anointed One.

## μεστός
**μεστός, ή, όν** full, filled with.

## μεστόω
**μεστόω** I fill.

## μετά
**μετά** (a) gen: with, in company with, (b) acc: (1) behind, beyond, after, of place, (2) after, of time, with nouns, neut. of adjectives.

## μεταβαίνω
**μεταβαίνω** I change my place (abode), leave, depart, remove, pass over.

## μεταβάλλομαι
**μεταβάλλω** I change; mid: I change my mind.

## μετάγω
**μετάγω** (usually: transfer, transport, and met: to a better mind), I turn about, change the position of; pass: I am brought back.

## μεταδίδωμι
**μεταδίδωμι** (lit: I offer by way of change, offer so that a change of owner is produced), I share; sometimes merely: I impart, bestow.

## μετάθεσις
**μετάθεσις, εως, ἡ** (a) change, transformation, (b) removal.

## μεταίρω
**μεταίρω** I change my position, depart, remove.

## μετακαλέομαι
**μετακαλέω** mid: I summon to myself, send for.

## μετακινέω
**μετακινέω** trans: I move away, dislodge, remove.

## μεταλαμβάνω
**μεταλαμβάνω** (a) with gen: I take a share (part) of, share in, partake of, (b) with acc: I take after (later) or take instead.

## μετάλημψις
**μετάληψις, εως, ἡ** participation, sharing in, receiving.

## μεταλλάσσω
**μεταλλάσσω** I change, transform, alter, exchange.

## μεταμέλομαι
**μεταμέλομαι** (lit: I change one care or interest for another), I change my mind (generally for the better), repent, regret.

## μεταμορφόομαι
**μεταμορφόω** I transform, transfigure.

## μετανοέω
**μετανοέω** I repent, change my mind, change the inner man (particularly with reference to acceptance of the will of God), repent.

## μετάνοια
**μετάνοια, ας, ἡ** repentance, a change of mind, change in the inner man.

## μεταξύ
**μεταξύ** meanwhile, afterwards, between.

## μεταπέμπομαι
**μεταπέμπω** I send for, summon.

## μεταστρέφω
**μεταστρέφω** I turn, change, corrupt, pervert.

## μετασχηματίζω
**μετασχηματίζω** I change the outward appearance (the dress, the form of presentment) of something, transfigure; I adapt.

## μετατίθημι
**μετατίθημι** (a) I transfer, mid: I go over to another party, desert, (b) I change.

## μετατρέπω
**μεταστρέφω** I turn, change, corrupt, pervert.

## μετέπειτα
**μετέπειτα** afterwards.

## μετέχω
**μετέχω** I have a share of, participate in, share, partake of, am a member of.

## μετεωρίζομαι
**μετεωρίζομαι** I am suspended, anxious.

## μετοικεσία
**μετοικεσία, ας, ἡ** change of abode, migration, deportation.

## μετοικίζω

## μετοχή
**μετοχή, ῆς, ἡ** sharing, partnership, fellowship.

## μέτοχος
**μέτοχος, ου, ὁ** a sharer, partner, associate.

## μετρέω

## μετρητής
**μετρητής, οῦ, ὁ** a measure, amphora, about 39.39 liters or 8.75 gallons.

## μετριοπαθέω
**μετριοπαθέω** I bear gently with, have compassion.

## μετρίως
**μετρίως** moderately, greatly, exceedingly.

## μέτρον
**μέτρον, ου, τό** a measure, whether lineal or cubic; a measuring rod.

## μέτωπον
**μέτωπον, ου, τό** the forehead, front.

**μέχρι** as far as, until, even to.

## μήγε
**μή** not, lest.

## μηδαμῶς
**μηδαμῶς** by no means, not at all.

## μηδέ
**μηδέ** and not, not even, neither...nor.

## μηδείς
**μηδείς, μηδεμία, μηδέν** no one, none, nothing.

## μηδέποτε
**μηδέποτε** not at any time, never.

## μηδέπω
**μηδέπω** not yet.

## Μῆδος
**Μῆδος, ου, ὁ** a Mede, a Median, from east of Assyria.

## μηκέτι
**μηκέτι** no longer, no more.

## μῆκος
**μῆκος, ους, τό** length.

## μηκύνομαι

## μηλωτή
**μηλωτή, ῆς, ἡ** a sheep's (sometimes pig's) hide, sheepskin.

## μήν

## μηνύω

## μήποτε

## μήπω
**μήπω** not yet.

## μηρός

## μήτε
